Exec wall 157.7 s; `gpu_active`≈100.9 s (WASB); `bytes_out`=`report.json`+`intervals.csv`. Output `gs://…/_valclose/v1-skipval/outputs/8fe6b719…/`, `status=success`, 13 segments.

Re-val wall **69.7 s** vs the OLD image's 169.3 s = **2.43× on identical L4 hardware**, and the **decision is byte-identical** (11.9 < 20.0) — #512 is pure speed, zero behavior change. (This run also shows *why* #513 is needed: absent the skip, the worker's default `min_blur=20` still rejects the clip the control-plane passed at 8.0.)

70	### 4b. Off-box #512 A/B — codec/resolution breadth (16-core local box, cv2 4.13.0, warm, `run_all`)
71	
72	Isolated clones at `23c1868` (OLD, per-sample seeks) vs `f5c9dd9` (NEW, shared pass); identical sample indices → a fair A/B. **Every validator decision is identical OLD↔NEW** on every clip (no PASS/FAIL drift from the fix).
73	
74	| Clip | codec / res | OLD (warm) | NEW (warm) | Speedup | decisions OLD↔NEW |
75	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
76	| Boxhill_Doubles | 1080p H.264 | 10.91 s | 5.60 s | **1.95×** | 6/6 identical (all PASS) |
77	| GX010128 | 4K HEVC (45298 fr) | 35.76 s | 11.54 s | **3.10×** | 6/6 identical (all PASS) |
78	| Video 1 | 5.3K 10-bit HEVC | 173.20 s | 53.62 s | **3.23×** | 6/6 identical (all PASS†) |
79	
80	† On this Windows/cv2 box the 10-bit-HEVC decode yields a Laplacian variance **above** 20 so blur PASSES off-box, whereas the Linux serving stack measures **11.9** and rejects. That platform decode-stack difference is **orthogonal to #512** (which changes only *which frames are sampled*, identically in both trees); the authoritative blur verdict and the real speedup come from the L4/prod (§4a). Off-box contributes only the OLD↔NEW **ratio**, which is decode-stack-independent.
81	
82	The win grows with per-frame decode cost (random-seek re-decode is what #512 removes), and it **triangulates** with the real-L4 Video 1 A/B (2.43×): the off-box ratios bound the control-plane projection.
83

92	## 6. Honest limits / not-verified
93	- **Live end-to-end CUJ is owner-gated.** `POST /api/process` on rev 00018 requires a Cloudflare-Access JWT (`edge_auth_enabled=true`; a direct call returns **401** `missing Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion header`). So the two ⏳ cells — the **live control-plane validation wall on rev 00018** and the **stitched reel** — need the owner to drive one upload (or hand over a CF-Access JWT / service token). Everything else here is measured on the real serving infra. **Recipe to close them:** upload "Video 1 - Badminton.MP4" via the SPA (compute=cloud), then `gcloud logging read '…service_name="rally-control-plane"…("Running validations" OR "passed checks")'` for the wall, and confirm the dispatched worker exec logs `validation SKIPPED` + emits rallies + the reel compiles. Expect CP validation ~2–3 min and a PASS.
94	- **#513's worker win is shown via a dispatch-faithful direct exec**, not a control-plane-initiated dispatch (same auth gate). The `--skip-validation` flag, its emission in `orchestration._maybe_dispatch_remote`, and its handling in `worker.cmd_infer` are unit-tested on master; Run A exercises the exact worker code path with the exact flag.
95	- **Rallies, not a reel.** Run A used `skip_ai_handoff=true` (secret-free) so the 13 "rallies" are local candidate windows (no Gemini refine, no stitched mp4) — this validates decode+detect+windowing end-to-end and that the clip **passes**; the reel stitch is the owner-CUJ step above. The 13 segments are its inputs.
96	- **Off-box absolute times are from a Windows 16-core box (cv2/FFmpeg), not the 2-vCPU Linux control-plane** — so the **ratios** transfer (the change is algorithmic: seeks→shared forward-hybrid pass), the absolutes don't, and (per †) the 10-bit blur *verdict* can differ; the control-plane absolute is anchored to the prod 412 s baseline, not the local numbers. Video 1 off-box is a cross-check of the authoritative real-L4 2.43×.
97	- **#515** (crash-before-marker fast-fail) is NOT in this image (`ef41473` post-dates `f5c9dd9`); it's unrelated to validation latency and out of scope for this closure.
98

101

103	✅ **CLOSED — #512 and #513 are proven on live cloud-serving hardware.** #513 converts the motivating soft-focus 5.3K clip from a post-dispatch FAIL into a 13-rally PASS by eliminating the worker's redundant re-validation (169 s + wrongful blur-reject → 0 s); #512 speeds the frame-sampling validators **2.43× on the real L4** and **1.95–3.23× off-box across codecs**, with **zero decision drift** OLD↔NEW on every clip. The only residual is the live control-plane validation wall on rev 00018 (owner-gated, projected ~2–3 min from ~6m52s) — its components are all measured. Validation-latency work is done.

### 50. assistant (2026-07-08T04:22:08.818Z)

Ah, a **new** (different) video actually resolves my caching worry cleanly — that's the best choice. Here's why, plus one caveat so the run gives us something useful.

**Why a new video is clean:** the code I just read confirms three short-circuits, all keyed on the *video_id* (= MD5 of the file bytes):
- **whole-pipeline cache** (`orchestration.py:781`) — if a video_id is already `processed` with cached segments and `force≠true`, it returns cached segments with **no validation, no dispatch, no reel**.
- **validation-result cache** (`orchestration.py:819`, #518) — reuses the stored verdict, skipping the validator suite (this is the one that would have eaten cell 1).
- A brand-new clip has a **new video_id**, so it misses all of them → I get a genuine `Running validations for …` → `Video passed checks` wall, a real dispatch, and a real reel. It even *improves* on the report's #513 proof, which §6 admits was a "dispatch-faithful direct exec, not a control-plane-initiated dispatch" — a live SPA run finally exercises `_maybe_dispatch_remote` → worker end-to-end.

**One caveat — pick a heavy clip if you can.** The validation wall scales with resolution/length/codec. The report's baseline was **412 s on the 5.3K 10-bit HEVC Video 1**. So:
- A **heavy clip (4K+/long, ideally HEVC)** → a substantial wall that actually demonstrates #512 live, and is roughly comparable to the projection.
- A **short 1080p clip** → validates in a few seconds; still proves the live pipeline + reel + `validation SKIPPED` dispatch, but won't showcase the speed *magnitude*.

Either way I'll measure exactly what lands and frame the report update honestly (it'll be a *new-clip* live-CUJ datapoint on rev 00019, not a re-measurement of Video 1's 412 s — I won't overclaim apples-to-apples).

### 114. user (2026-07-08T04:44:27.153Z)

1	"""Leak-scan guard — refuse to commit obvious PII / attribution leaks into the code surface.
2	
3	A *structural* CI guard (deliberately NOT a name detector): it scans the TRACKED text files under
4	the code/data roots for
5	
6	  (a) a 32-hex MD5 token — the ``video_id`` shape (``backend/utils/hashing.py::compute_video_id``);
7	      16-hex ``fx_`` fixture slugs and 12-hex ``gt_hash`` values do NOT match, by design.
8	  (b) a personal absolute user path — ``C:\\Users\\<name>``, ``/home/<name>/``, ``/Users/<name>/`` —
9	      where ``<name>`` is a real username (documented placeholders like ``<name>``/``<user>`` are OK).
10	
11	Optionally, given a PRIVATE (gitignored) name-list via ``--names`` (default ``<root>/.leakscan_private``
12	if present), it ALSO flags those tokens — so the owner can scan for real player/venue names locally
13	while CI runs only the name-free structural guard (you can't commit a name-list to scan for).
14	
15	A line carrying ``# leakscan-allow`` is skipped (escape hatch for a legitimate token, e.g. an MD5
16	test-vector). Run ``python -m tools.leak_scan`` (exit 1 on any finding). SCOPE = the shipped-code
17	surface (``backend/ training/ scripts/ tools/ eval_baselines/``); ``tests/`` (legit example
18	fixtures), ``docs/``, and gitignored ``scratch/``/``output/`` are NOT scanned (scan those locally
19	with ``--names`` against a private list if desired).
20	"""
21	
22	from __future__ import annotations
23	
24	import argparse
25	import re
26	import subprocess
27	import sys
28	from pathlib import Path
29	from typing import List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple
30	
31	#: Shipped-code + committed-data surfaces scanned. NOT ``tests/`` (fixtures legitimately embed
32	#: example paths/usernames/MD5s — noise for a structural guard; scan those locally with --names),
33	#: and NOT ``docs/``/``scratch/``/``output/`` (intentional operator notes).
34	SCAN_ROOTS: Tuple[str, ...] = (
35	    "backend",
36	    "training",
37	    "scripts",
38	    "tools",
39	    "eval_baselines",
40	)
41	
42	#: This tool + its test legitimately contain sample patterns — never scan them.
43	SELF_EXCLUDE: Tuple[str, ...] = ("tools/leak_scan.py", "tests/test_leak_scan.py")
44	
45	#: A run of EXACTLY 32 hex chars (the MD5 video_id shape). The negative look-around means a 16-hex
46	#: ``fx_`` slug, a 12-hex ``gt_hash``, or a 40/64-hex SHA do NOT match.
47	_MD5_RE = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9a-fA-F])[0-9a-fA-F]{32}(?![0-9a-fA-F])")
48	_WIN_USER_RE = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z]:[\\/]+Users[\\/]+([A-Za-z0-9._-]+)")
49	_NIX_USER_RE = re.compile(r"/(?:home|Users)/([A-Za-z0-9._-]+)/")
50	
51	#: Tokens that are a DOCUMENTED placeholder / generic example, not a real username — so a doc'd
52	#: path like ``C:\\Users\\x`` or ``/home/u/`` does not trip the guard.
53	_PLACEHOLDERS = frozenset(
54	    {
55	        "<name>",
56	        "<user>",
57	        "<username>",
58	        "<you>",
59	        "you",
60	        "youruser",
61	        "yourname",
62	        "user",
63	        "username",
64	        "x",
65	        "u",
66	        "a",
67	        "b",
68	        "c",
69	        "n",
70	        "foo",
71	        "bar",
72	        "baz",
73	        "test",
74	        "testuser",
75	        "tmp",
76	        "home",
77	        "example",
78	    }
79	)
80	
81	_ALLOW = "# leakscan-allow"
82	_TEXT_EXT = {
83	    ".py",
84	    ".json",
85	    ".txt",
86	    ".csv",
87	    ".yml",
88	    ".yaml",
89	    ".cfg",
90	    ".ini",
91	    ".toml",
92	    ".md",
93	}
94	
95	
96	def _tracked_files(root: Path) -> List[str]:
97	    """The tracked files under SCAN_ROOTS (via ``git ls-files`` so only committed paths are scanned)."""
98	    out = subprocess.run(
99	        ["git", "ls-files", *SCAN_ROOTS], cwd=str(root), capture_output=True, text=True
100	    )
101	    return [f.strip() for f in out.stdout.splitlines() if f.strip()]
102	
103	
104	def _load_names(path: Optional[Path]) -> List[str]:
105	    if path is None or not path.is_file():
106	        return []
107	    return [
108	        ln.strip()
109	        for ln in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").splitlines()
110	        if ln.strip() and not ln.lstrip().startswith("#")
111	    ]
112	
113	
114	def scan_text(text: str, *, names: Sequence[str] = ()) -> List[Tuple[int, str]]:
115	    """Return ``(lineno, reason)`` findings for one file's text (1-based line numbers)."""
116	    findings: List[Tuple[int, str]] = []
117	    lnames = [n.lower() for n in names if n]
118	    for i, line in enumerate(text.splitlines(), start=1):
119	        if _ALLOW in line:
120	            continue
121	        if _MD5_RE.search(line):
122	            findings.append((i, "32-hex token (video_id / MD5 shape)"))
123	        for m in _WIN_USER_RE.finditer(line):
124	            if m.group(1).lower() not in _PLACEHOLDERS:
125	                findings.append(
126	                    (i, f"personal Windows user path (…Users\\{m.group(1)}…)")
127	                )
128	                break
129	        for m in _NIX_USER_RE.finditer(line):
130	            if m.group(1).lower() not in _PLACEHOLDERS:
131	                findings.append((i, f"personal POSIX user path (…/{m.group(1)}/…)"))
132	                break
133	        low = line.lower()
134	        for n in lnames:
135	            if n in low:
136	                findings.append((i, f"private name-list token {n!r}"))
137	                break
138	    return findings
139	
140	
141	def scan_repo(
142	    root: Path, *, names_file: Optional[Path] = None
143	) -> List[Tuple[str, int, str]]:
144	    """Scan the tracked code-surface files under ``root``; return ``(relpath, lineno, reason)``."""
145	    names = _load_names(names_file)
146	    results: List[Tuple[str, int, str]] = []
147	    for rel in _tracked_files(root):
148	        if rel in SELF_EXCLUDE or Path(rel).suffix.lower() not in _TEXT_EXT:
149	            continue
150	        try:
151	            text = (root / rel).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
152	        except OSError:
153	            continue
154	        for lineno, reason in scan_text(text, names=names):
155	            results.append((rel, lineno, reason))
156	    return results
157	
158	
159	def main(argv: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None) -> int:
160	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(
161	        description="Leak-scan guard: 32-hex MD5 + personal paths (+ optional private name-list)."
162	    )
163	    ap.add_argument(
164	        "--root",
165	        type=Path,
166	        default=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1],
167	        help="repo root (default: the parent of tools/)",
168	    )
169	    ap.add_argument(
170	        "--names",
171	        type=Path,
172	        default=None,
173	        help="optional gitignored private name-list (default: <root>/.leakscan_private if present)",
174	    )
175	    args = ap.parse_args(list(argv) if argv is not None else None)
176	    root: Path = args.root
177	    names_file = args.names if args.names is not None else (root / ".leakscan_private")
178	    findings = scan_repo(root, names_file=names_file)
179	    if not findings:
180	        print(
181	            "[leak-scan] clean — no MD5 / personal-path / private-name leaks in the code surface."
182	        )
183	        return 0
184	    print(
185	        f"[leak-scan] {len(findings)} potential leak(s) "
186	        f"(add '# leakscan-allow' on a line to suppress a legitimate one):",
187	        file=sys.stderr,
188	    )
189	    for rel, lineno, reason in findings:
190	        print(f"  {rel}:{lineno}: {reason}", file=sys.stderr)
191	    return 1
192	
193	
194	if __name__ == "__main__":
195	    raise SystemExit(main())
196

### 116. user (2026-07-08T04:44:43.225Z)

1	"""Markdown internal-link checker — fail CI on a broken internal link in a LIVE doc.
2	
3	Scans tracked ``*.md`` files (via ``git ls-files``) and, for each ``[text](dest)`` / ``![alt](dest)``
4	link whose destination is INTERNAL (a repo path and/or a ``#anchor``), verifies that
5	
6	  - the target file exists (resolved relative to the linking file, or the repo root for ``/abs``), and
7	  - any ``#anchor`` matches a GitHub-style heading slug in the target markdown file.
8	
9	SKIPPED: external links (``http(s)``/``mailto``/``tel``/``ftp``/protocol-relative ``//``), links inside
10	fenced code blocks, and any file under ``docs/archives/**`` — archived docs are frozen snapshots whose
11	relative links are historical-by-design (see ``docs/DOC_STATUS.md`` §2). Mirrors ``tools/leak_scan.py``:
12	run ``python -m tools.check_md_links`` (exit 1 on any broken internal link in a live doc).
13	"""
14	
15	from __future__ import annotations
16	
17	import argparse
18	import re
19	import subprocess
20	import sys
21	from pathlib import Path
22	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Sequence, Set, Tuple
23	
24	#: Source docs under these prefixes are NOT scanned — archived = frozen snapshots whose relative
25	#: links are historical-by-design (DOC_STATUS.md §2 step 5).
26	EXCLUDE_PREFIXES: Tuple[str, ...] = ("docs/archives/",)
27	#: This tool + its test legitimately embed sample/broken links — never scan them.
28	SELF_EXCLUDE: Tuple[str, ...] = ("tools/check_md_links.py", "tests/test_check_md_links.py")
29	
30	_LINK_RE = re.compile(r"!?\[[^\]]*\]\(\s*([^)]+?)\s*\)")
31	_HEADING_RE = re.compile(r"^(#{1,6})\s+(.*?)\s*#*\s*$")
32	_FENCE_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*(?:```|~~~)")
33	#: a scheme (``http:``, ``mailto:``, …) or protocol-relative ``//`` ⇒ external, not our concern.
34	_EXTERNAL_RE = re.compile(r"^(?:[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.\-]*:|//)")
35	
36	
37	def slugify(heading: str) -> str:
38	    """GitHub-style heading → anchor slug (matches `github-slugger`): lowercase, drop punctuation
39	    (keep word chars / spaces / hyphens), then replace EACH space with a hyphen — crucially NOT
40	    collapsing, so a heading with removed punctuation (e.g. 'a / b' → 'a  b') yields '--' like GitHub."""
41	    s = re.sub(r"[^\w\s-]", "", heading.strip().lower())
42	    return s.replace(" ", "-")
43	
44	
45	def heading_slugs(text: str) -> Set[str]:
46	    """The set of GitHub anchor slugs for a markdown file's headings (with -1/-2 dedup suffixes)."""
47	    counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
48	    out: Set[str] = set()
49	    in_fence = False
50	    for line in text.splitlines():
51	        if _FENCE_RE.match(line):
52	            in_fence = not in_fence
53	            continue
54	        if in_fence:
55	            continue
56	        m = _HEADING_RE.match(line)
57	        if not m:
58	            continue
59	        base = slugify(m.group(2))
60	        n = counts.get(base, 0)

## 9. Update (2026-07-08) — live SPA CUJ closes the two owner-gated cells (on rev `00019`)

The two ⏳ cells above (the **live control-plane validation wall** and the **end-to-end reel**) were owner-gated on the Cloudflare-Access `POST /api/process`. On **2026-07-08** the owner drove one real SPA upload (compute=**Cloud**) end-to-end, so both are now measured on live serving infra. Mirrors the gpu-resident report's "§5 Update" append — the body above is unchanged; this section records what the live run added.

**Two honest deltas from the recipe** (state moved after this report's 2026-07-07 image cutover):
1. **Live rev rolled `00018` → `00019`.** Traffic is 100% on **`rally-control-plane-00019-96f`**, image `rally-worker@sha256:c6393d27b4ae…` (tag `:latest`), deployed 2026-07-07T17:06Z — ~51 min after the `00018`/`8045f54e` cutover §2 documents; the worker Job is on the same `:latest`=`c6393d27` digest (both surfaces match). It's an undocumented forward-bump, but #512 (`20862d7`) and #513 (`23c1868`) are ancestors of `origin/master` and `f5c9dd9` (the `00018` image src) is an ancestor of `c6393d27`'s master line, and the run **empirically** shows both fixes live (one-pass validation wall + worker `validation SKIPPED`). Measured on what is actually serving.
2. **A fresh clip, not Video 1.** The upload was **"First CUJ.MP4"** — **1.83 GiB** (1,960,043,219 B), source duration **174.17 s** (≈84 Mbps, 4K-class — *heavier* than Video 1's 1.41 GiB), video_id `2f31b45e771cc9fca179cc858ac62750`. So the live wall **corroborates** the Video-1 projection on a comparably-heavy clip; it is **not** a re-measurement of Video 1's 412 s→~170 s A/B (that stays a projection — a different clip has a different absolute wall).

**Live timeline (UTC, rev `00019`):**

**Cell 1 — control-plane validation AFTER (live):** **183 s (3m03s)** on rev `00019`, PASSED — the #512 shared-decode validators on the real 2-vCPU serving path, landing in the projected **2–3 min** band on a clip *heavier* than Video 1. **Bonus — #513 proof upgraded.** §6 flagged that #513's worker win was shown via a *dispatch-faithful direct exec*, not a control-plane-initiated dispatch. This live CUJ closes that: the control-plane's `_maybe_dispatch_remote` emitted `--skip-validation` on a genuine dispatch and the worker honored it (`validation SKIPPED`, 0 re-validation) — the full path, end-to-end.

### 140. assistant (2026-07-08T04:56:28.500Z)

## Problem
Observing a run today means scraping Cloud Run stdout **after the fact** via `gcloud logging read`, then hand-correlating timestamps across **three** separate streams — the control-plane service, the worker Job, and the in-process compiler — and there is **no UI-side signal at all**. Reconstructing the phase timeline for the 2026-07-08 live CUJ (upload → validate → dispatch → detect → ingest → compile) required manually diffing log timestamps across those streams.

## Ask
A **debug/trace mode** (config- or env-gated, **off by default**) where every pipeline step — and key UI actions — emits a **structured event** to a **low-latency sink**, so a run can be observed live and replayed:

- **Server phases:** upload (per-part / complete), validation (per-validator wall + verdict), dispatch (job/exec id + cold-start), worker (pull, skip-validation, WASB phase), ingest, compile (per-segment trim, concat, mirror-upload).
- **UI events:** upload start/complete, Process click, poll states, Compile click, download.
- **Event shape:** `{ts, run_id, video_id, phase, step, machine, wall_ms, meta}`.
- **Sink (configurable):**
  - GCS — one JSONL per run at `gs://<output_root>/_debug/<video_id>/trace.jsonl` (cheap, durable, replayable), and/or
  - a lightweight collector / Pub/Sub topic for **live** viewing (e.g. an `/api/debug/trace/<video_id>` SSE stream, or push to an existing observability service).
- Gate behind e.g. `observability.debug_trace: true` (+ sink target); never on by default (PII / volume).

## Why now
The live CUJ (`docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_validation-latency_2026-07-07.md` §9) had to be timed **by hand** across control-plane + worker + compiler logs; a first-class per-run trace (server **and** UI) would make the whole phase breakdown a single artifact and enable low-latency live observation.

_Filed from the 2026-07-08 live-CUJ observability gap._

### 142. assistant (2026-07-08T04:56:44.258Z)

## Problem
The **compile/stitch** phase runs **in-process on the CPU-only 2-vCPU control-plane** (`backend/orchestration.py::_execute_compile` → `_LOCAL_COMPUTE_LANE` ffmpeg). **Validation also runs on the control-plane.** On the 2026-07-08 live CUJ (rev `rally-control-plane-00019`, clip "First CUJ.MP4" — 1.83 GiB / 174.17 s / 4K-class):

| Phase | Machine | Wall |
|---|---|---|
| Validation (#512 shared-decode) | control-plane 2 vCPU | **183 s** |
| Detection (WASB) | L4 worker | 161.6 s |
| **Compile / stitch** (11 clips → ~80 s reel) | **control-plane 2 vCPU** | **~12m55s** |

Stitch was **~45–90 s per ~7 s clip** because the **per-clip branding watermark forces a full 4K re-encode** with no NVENC on 2 vCPU. So a single run pins the control-plane for **~16 min of heavy CPU** (validation + stitch), serialized by `_LOCAL_COMPUTE_LANE`. At even a handful of concurrent users the control-plane saturates and the API becomes unusable — estimated **>~5 concurrent users renders it useless**.

## Ask
1. **Move compile/stitch to the L4 worker** (hardware **NVENC** + 8 vCPU), the way detection already dispatches. The worker already fetches the source, and `_execute_compile` is designed to re-resolve in the worker (`_resolve_compile` is explicitly "run at SUBMIT **AND** in the WORKER"). Add a `kind='compile'` Cloud Run dispatch path.
2. **Make phase→machine placement configurable** — a per-phase target knob (`validation`, `segment/detect`, `compile/stitch` → `control_plane | cloud_run_l4 | <other>`) so phases can be relocated **by config, no code change**. Mirror the existing `deployment.compute_target` + `_maybe_dispatch_remote` forwarding pattern (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING).
3. **Cheaper watermark** — pre-rendered overlay / NVENC filter path so stitch isn't a full CPU re-encode (the dominant term above).

## Acceptance
- Compile can be dispatched to the L4 by config; control-plane stitch stays available as a config option.
- Flipping config moves validation / detect / compile between machines **without a code change**.
- Measured stitch wall for this reel drops from ~13 min (2 vCPU) to sub-minute-class on L4.

## Evidence
`docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_validation-latency_2026-07-07.md` §9 (2026-07-08 live CUJ). Compile timeline: `POST /api/compile` 04:37:26 → 11× per-clip 4K re-encode+watermark (~67 s each) → concat ~2 s → reel mirrored 04:50:21 (`first-cuj-ks-highlight-reel.mp4`, 581.8 MB).

_Filed from the 2026-07-08 live-CUJ phase breakdown._

Everything else here is measured on the real serving infra. **Recipe to close them:** upload "Video 1 - Badminton.MP4" via the SPA (compute=cloud), then `gcloud logging read '…service_name="rally-control-plane"…("Running validations" OR "passed checks")'` for the wall, and confirm the dispatched worker exec logs `validation SKIPPED` + emits rallies + the reel compiles. Expect CP validation ~2–3 min and a PASS.
94	- **#513's worker win is shown via a dispatch-faithful direct exec**, not a control-plane-initiated dispatch (same auth gate). 95	- **Rallies, not a reel.** Run A used `skip_ai_handoff=true` (secret-free) so the 13 "rallies" are local candidate windows (no Gemini refine, no stitched mp4) — this validates decode+detect+windowing end-to-end and that the clip **passes**; the reel stitch is the owner-CUJ step above. 96	- **Off-box absolute times are from a Windows 16-core box (cv2/FFmpeg), not the 2-vCPU Linux control-plane** — so the **ratios** transfer (the change is algorithmic: seeks→shared forward-hybrid pass), the absolutes don't, and (per †) the 10-bit blur *verdict* can differ; the control-plane absolute is anchored to the prod 412 s baseline, not the local numbers. 97	- **#515** (crash-before-marker fast-fail) is NOT in this image (`ef41473` post-dates `f5c9dd9`); it's unrelated to validation latency and out of scope for this closure.

102	## 8. Closure verdict
103	✅ **CLOSED — #512 and #513 are proven on live cloud-serving hardware.** #513 converts the motivating soft-focus 5.3K clip from a post-dispatch FAIL into a 13-rally PASS by eliminating the worker's redundant re-validation (169 s + wrongful blur-reject → 0 s); #512 speeds the frame-sampling validators **2.43× on the real L4** and **1.95–3.23× off-box across codecs**, with **zero decision drift** OLD↔NEW on every clip. ## 2. Complete phase breakdown

| # | Phase | Wall | Machine | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Upload** (80-part multipart) | **~2m55s** | your uplink | 1.83 GiB @ ~84 Mbps — *your bandwidth, not infra* |
| 2 | Accept → validation start (stage) | ~30s | control-plane | stage upload into serving bucket |
| 3 | **Validation** (#512 shared-decode) ✅ | **183s** | control-plane 2 vCPU | one-pass, PASSED |
| 4 | Dispatch → worker ready (cold start) | ~55s | Cloud Run Job | image pull + GCSFuse + torch/cuda init |
| 5 | **Worker skip-validation** (#513) ✅ | ~4s | L4 | 0 re-validation |
| 6 | Detection (WASB) | **161.6s** | L4 (cuda) | 15 candidate windows |
| 7 | Ingest + finalize | ~4s | control-plane | intervals→DB, success |
| — | *(you review, click Compile)* | ~62s idle | — | human-in-loop |
| 8 | **Compile / stitch** ❌ | **~12m55s** | control-plane 2 vCPU | 11 clips, **~67s/clip** |

**Server pipeline (process→success): 7m32s. Full CUJ (upload→reel): ~24m25s.**

**Wins:** #512 validation **183s** on a clip *heavier* than Video 1 (pre-#512 seeks were ~7-min class); #513 saved a redundant ~160s worker re-validation.
**Losses:** the stitch — see below.

---

## 3. Why stitching took *too* long (confirmed)

You're right, and here's the mechanism: each of the 11 clips took **45–90 s** to produce for a **~7 s** clip. That's a **full 4K re-encode per clip to burn in the branding watermark**, on the **CPU-only 2-vCPU control-plane with no NVENC**. The concat itself was ~2 s (stream-copy) — it's the per-clip watermark re-encode that dominates. Output reel is 581 MB for ~80 s (~58 Mbps) — also a symptom of the heavy 4K re-encode.

Your scaling concern is exactly right: validation (183 s) **and** stitch (775 s) both run on the control-plane, serialized by a mutex (`_LOCAL_COMPUTE_LANE`) → **~16 min of heavy control-plane CPU per run**. A few concurrent users saturate it.

---

## 4. Cost heuristic for this run

| Component | Estimate | Note |
|---|---|---|
| L4 detect exec (~222 s) | **$0.15–0.55** | dominant; rate-dependent |
| Control-plane CPU (validation + stitch, ~960 s @ 2 vCPU) | ~$0.05 | cheap in $, but the concurrency killer |
| Reel egress (581 MB, if you downloaded it) | ~$0.07 | GCS→internet, asia |
| Storage + ops | < $0.02 | 1.83 GiB upload + reel, short-lived |
| Gemini | $0 | skip_ai_handoff=true |
| **Total** | **≈ $0.3–0.7** | ~one L4 exec, as projected |

---

## 5. Issues filed + PR

- **[#521 — perf/scaling: run compile/stitch on the L4 + config-driven phase placement](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/521)** (`bug`,`enhancement`) — this is your "run stitch on L4" + "make phases movable by config" ask, backed by today's numbers. Covers: dispatch `kind='compile'` to the L4 (NVENC), a per-phase `control_plane | cloud_run_l4 | …` config knob, and a cheaper watermark path.

### 186. assistant (2026-07-08T05:10:38.428Z)

Good catch to question it — **my label was wrong**, in two ways. Here's what that ~30 s gap actually is, from the logs + code.

## What really happens (04:28:52 → 04:29:22)

```
04:28:51.162  POST /api/upload/c6d256da…/complete 200   ← the 1.83 GiB file is ALREADY in the bucket here
04:28:52.200  POST /api/process 202 Accepted            ← returns async job_id 4edb3c40…
04:28:53–04:29:20  GET /api/jobs/4edb3c40… 200 (×7)      ← just the SPA polling job status; no server work logged
04:29:22.789  Running validations for gs://…/First CUJ.MP4
```

The upload finished landing in the serving bucket at **04:28:51** (the multipart `/complete`). So nothing gets "staged into the bucket" in that gap — it's already there. The gap is the **opposite direction**.

In `backend/orchestration.py::_execute_processing` (line 748), before it can validate:
```python
local_video = storage.acquire_local_input(req.video_path, …)   # fetch the gs:// object DOWN to /tmp
video_id    = _main.compute_video_id(local_video)              # MD5 the whole file → 2f31b45e…
```
Both read the **entire 1.83 GiB**: `acquire_local_input` downloads the bucket object to the control-plane's local scratch (the validators decode a local file, not gs://), and `compute_video_id` hashes it. At ~60 MB/s that download is ~30 s — which is the gap. It's **unlogged** (no INFO line), which is why only the SPA's job-polls show up — exactly the blind spot **#520** is meant to fix.

## Corrected label
That row should read: **"control-plane fetches the uploaded clip from the bucket → local scratch + hashes it (video_id), before the validators can decode."** A download + hash, not a stage-to-bucket. (My "stage upload into serving bucket" was just wrong — apologies.)

## Bonus insight it exposes
The same 1.83 GiB gets **pulled down twice**: once to the **control-plane** here (to validate + hash), then again to the **worker** at `04:33:20 [worker] pulled …First CUJ.MP4 → in.mp4` (to detect). If validation moves onto the L4 too (part of **#521**'s phase-placement idea), the control-plane wouldn't need to pull the full file at all — it could stay a thin dispatcher. Worth noting on that issue; want me to add it as a comment on #521?

261	                        f"Part exceeds the {part_bytes // (1024 * 1024)} MB per-part limit."
262	                    )
263	                    break
264	                if st["bytes"] + received > max_bytes:
265	                    overflow = (
266	                        f"Upload exceeds the {max_bytes // (1024 * 1024)} MB total limit."
267	                    )
268	                    break
269	                fh.write(chunk)
270	    if overflow:
271	        _abort_upload(upload_id)
272	        raise HTTPException(
273	            status_code=413, detail=overflow + " Upload aborted — re-init to retry."
274	        )
275	
276	    with _uploads_lock:
277	        st["parts"] += 1
278	        st["bytes"] += received
279	    return {
280	        "upload_id": upload_id,
281	        "received_parts": st["parts"],
282	        "received_bytes": st["bytes"],
283	    }

### 193. assistant (2026-07-08T05:13:01.912Z)

Confirmed from the code — you're right, and my "download from the bucket" framing needs the *reason* corrected too. Here's the accurate picture.

## Yes — the upload streams straight into the bucket

In hosted/cloud-serving mode (`storage.input_backend=gcs`, `input_root` set), `backend/api/uploads.py` does **not** buffer to local disk:
- `upload_init` → `_staging_target()` picks the GCS root and opens a **streaming writer** to `<input_root>/uploads/<upload_id>/<name>` (uploads.py:175-177).
- each `PUT …/part/N` streams that part **straight through to GCS** (`st["writer"].write`, uploads.py:253) — the docstring is explicit: *"the assembled file never touches local disk/tmpfs."* This was a deliberate fix (**#480/#471**): the 4 GiB-RAM `/tmp` control plane **OOM'd on a 2.68 GB upload**, so now instance memory bounds a *chunk*, not the video.
- an **incremental MD5 accumulates as it streams**, so `/complete` returns `{path: gs://…, video_id: <streamed md5>}` (uploads.py:305-317) — the id is computed *during* upload, no re-read.

So at `04:28:51` (`/complete`) the file is already in the bucket **and** the video_id is already known.

## Which means my gap label was wrong twice

Not "stage into the bucket" (already there), and not "MD5 the file" (already hashed at upload). What the ~30 s gap **actually** is — and it's a consequence of the direct-to-bucket design:

Because the upload kept **no local copy**, `_execute_processing` has nothing on disk. The validators decode a *local* file, so at orchestration.py:748-751 it does:
```python
local_video = storage.acquire_local_input(req.video_path, …)  # req.video_path = gs://…  → FETCH the 1.83 GiB back DOWN to /tmp
video_id    = _main.compute_video_id(local_video)             # re-hash the local copy (redundant — upload already had it)
```
So the gap = **re-downloading the clip from the bucket to control-plane scratch** (+ a redundant re-hash + async job pickup). All silent — only the SPA's `GET /api/jobs/…` polls appear (→ exactly the #520 blind spot).

## The real insight

The same 1.83 GiB moves **three times**: streamed **up** (upload → bucket), pulled **down** once (control-plane, to validate), pulled **down** again (worker `04:33:20 [worker] pulled …`, to detect). The direct-to-bucket upload is what *forces* the control-plane's re-download.

Two cleanups fall out of this, both on **#521**'s phase-placement idea:
1. Move validation onto the L4 (which already downloads the file for detection) → the control-plane never pulls the full clip at all, and the ~30 s gap disappears.
2. `/api/process` could trust the upload's streamed `video_id` and skip the redundant re-hash (the download would still be needed *only if* validation stays on the CP).

Want me to add this "clip downloaded 3× / redundant re-hash" note as a comment on #521?

### 196. assistant (2026-07-08T05:40:24.280Z)

**Epic / investigation.** Related: #521 (compile→L4 + config-driven phase placement), #520 (step-level tracing). Motivated by the 2026-07-08 live-CUJ data-movement finding (see #521 comment).

## Vision
Make the **L4 worker the primary compute for the whole pipeline** — upload → validate → segment → stitch all on the L4's local disk — and **shrink the control-plane to the smallest possible surface** (auth, routing/dispatch, DB, SPA serving, job status). Today the control-plane does heavy CPU work (validation ~183 s + stitch ~13 min on 2 vCPU) and the source clip is moved **3×** per run (upload→bucket, bucket→CP-scratch to validate, bucket→worker-scratch to detect) — see the #521 comment.

## Proposed direction (owner)
1. **Upload directly to the L4** (write to L4 local disk) instead of streaming to the bucket, so the pipeline never re-downloads.
2. **Async copy-to-bucket for durability** — while the video is being segmented, a background `gsutil`/`storage.copy` mirrors the local file to GCS. Accepts a small durability window (data briefly only on L4 disk) in exchange for eliminating the round-trips. The L4's local disk is reliable enough that this is an acceptable trade.
3. **Control-plane residual = thin router**: Cloudflare-Access auth, dispatch, DB writes (`play_segments`/video records), SPA + job-status. No heavy decode/encode.

## Hard question the investigation MUST resolve first (compute topology)
The current L4 is a Cloud Run **Job** (batch, no inbound HTTP, spun up per-dispatch). "Upload directly to the L4" needs an **inbound endpoint on the GPU box**, which a Job can't provide. Options — evaluate cost/latency/ops for each:
- **A. Cloud Run Service with GPU** (scale-to-zero or min-instances) hosting the upload + pipeline. Cold-start vs. warm-idle-GPU cost.
- **B. GCE L4 VM** (persistent, reliable PD) hosting upload + pipeline. Persistent GPU cost, but a real reliable disk (matches the owner's "reliable disk" point).
- **C. Hybrid**: control-plane presigns/redirects the upload to a just-spun-up L4 (service/VM), pipeline runs there, async copy to bucket.
- **Key tension to quantify:** uploads can take **minutes** (user bandwidth — the 2026-07-08 run uploaded 1.83 GiB in ~2m55s). Uploading *onto* the L4 means an **expensive GPU sits idle during the whole upload**, whereas today upload hits the cheap control-plane/bucket and the L4 only runs for the ~4-min detect. Does the round-trip saving beat the idle-GPU cost? A middle ground: upload to a **cheap L4-adjacent disk / non-GPU sidecar** co-located with the L4, or keep the bucket upload but co-locate compute so the download is LAN-fast. The investigation should produce the numbers.

## Concrete near-term wins (low risk — can land independently, overlap #521)
- **Move validation onto the L4** (it already downloads the clip for detect) → control-plane stops pulling the full file; the ~30 s silent pre-validation gap disappears.
- **Trust the upload's streamed `video_id`** in `/api/process` (`backend/api/uploads.py` already computes the MD5 incrementally during the stream) → drop the redundant re-hash in `backend/orchestration.py:751`.

## Deliverables
1. A **design doc** under `docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/` that resolves the topology question (A/B/C) with a cost/latency/reliability comparison, defines the minimal control-plane surface, specifies the async copy-to-bucket durability model (failure/resume semantics + the exposure window), and lays out a **phased migration** (start from the #521 config-driven phase-placement so each move is a config flip with a bucket-mediated fallback).
2. Land the two near-term wins above (guarded by config; tests + full CI green).
3. Phase the risky rearchitecture behind the design doc — do not rush upload-to-L4 before the topology/cost question is answered.

## Acceptance
- Design doc merged with a clear recommendation + phased plan + reliability analysis.
- Near-term wins landed (validation-on-L4 config path avoids the CP download; redundant re-hash removed), full suite + ruff + mypy + leak_scan + check_md_links green, coverage ≥ floor.
- Everything reversible by config (bucket-mediated path stays as fallback).

## Pricing Per Second

## Scale to Zero Capability

Instances of a Cloud Run service that has been configured to use GPU can scale down to zero for cost savings when not in use. Cloud Run automatically scales your GPU instances down to zero when no requests are received, eliminating idle costs.

## Key Features

You are only charged for the GPU resources you consume, down to the second. Google provides NVIDIA L4 GPUs with 24 GB of GPU memory (VRAM), which is separate from the instance memory.

Cloud Run's GPU support also offers rapid startup and scaling with instances starting up in under 5 seconds, making it suitable for bursty or unpredictable workloads where you want on-demand scaling without managing VM lifecycles.

### 211. assistant (2026-07-08T05:48:13.956Z)

Verified enough to be concrete (exact asia-southeast1 cents are behind a paywall, so I'll give ranges with the right caveat). Here's the real distinction.

## "Is it always on?" — that's the whole difference

| | **Cloud Run Job** (today) | **Cloud Run Service + GPU** | **GCE L4 VM** (`g2-standard-8`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lifecycle | spun up per-dispatch, dies after | scale-to-zero *or* min-instances=1 | **runs 24/7 until you stop it** |
| Billed for | only the ~4-min exec | GPU-seconds while serving (0 at idle if scale-to-zero) | **every second it's in RUNNING state, busy or idle** |
| Idle cost | $0 | $0 (scale-to-zero) / full (min-instances) | **full rate** unless you stop it |
| Inbound HTTP | ❌ none (batch only) | ✅ yes | ✅ yes (you own the port) |
| Cold start | ~55 s seen (image pull + torch/GCSFuse) | "<5 s" for GPU alloc, but really tens of s with your image | **none** — always warm |
| Local disk | ephemeral tmp | ephemeral tmp | ✅ **persistent disk** (durable, survives reboot) |
| Ops burden | none (managed) | none (managed) | **you own** OS/patching/drivers/health |
| HA | Google-managed | Google-managed | **single VM = single point of failure** (mitigate w/ MIG) |

So **a VM is "always on" by choice** — it bills the moment it's RUNNING and keeps billing while idle. You *can* stop/start it (compute billing pauses, disk keeps billing), but a stop→start is a ~30–60 s boot + your app init, so for an "upload lands here" design you'd keep it warm during active hours.

## The numbers (verified today, asia-southeast1 ≈ ranges)

- **GCE `g2-standard-8`** (1× L4, 8 vCPU, 32 GB — *exactly* your current worker shape): on-demand **~$0.85/hr** in cheap regions → **~$0.95–1.05/hr in asia-southeast1** → **~$620–770/mo always-on**. **Spot ~$0.28–0.35/hr** (but preemptible — bad for an upload endpoint). **CUD: 1-yr ≈ −37%, 3-yr ≈ −55%.** Plus a persistent SSD disk (~$0.10–0.17/GB-mo) for the video staging.
- **Cloud Run L4**: **~$0.67/hr** GPU (no zonal redundancy) to **~$1.05/hr** (with it), **plus** separate vCPU+memory charges. For an *always-warm* 8-vCPU/32-GB service those add up, so an always-on Cloud Run L4 typically lands **at or above the VM's bundled rate** — Cloud Run's advantage is entirely **scale-to-zero** ($0 idle).

## The insight: it's not the hourly rate, it's utilization

The hourly rates are *close* (~$0.7–1.05/hr both). The gap is idle time:
- **Bursty / low usage** → Cloud Run scale-to-zero wins (you pay ~$0.5/run, $0 idle).
- **Your upload-on-L4 vision inflates "active" time** — the GPU sits idle through a multi-minute upload — which pushes you toward near-always-on. **And once you're paying for always-on, the VM is the better always-on:** similar-or-cheaper hourly (much cheaper with spot/CUD), *plus* a persistent reliable disk, *plus* a real inbound endpoint, *plus* full control — none of which Cloud Run scale-to-zero gives you. A Cloud Run min-instances service gets you warm + inbound but **still no persistent disk** and usually costs *more*.

Rough break-even: an always-on VM (~$620–770/mo) beats per-run Cloud Run somewhere north of **~40–50 runs/day** — but the upload-on-L4 design lengthens each Cloud Run "run" (upload + detect + stitch), so the crossover comes *much* sooner. That's the number the #524 design doc should pin against your real expected load.

## Your tunneling instinct is right — and cleanest on a VM

Because you already use **Cloudflare Access**, the tidy answer is a **Cloudflare Tunnel (`cloudflared`) on the VM**: the browser still hits `api.khelsutra.guru`, CF routes through the tunnel to the VM — **no public IP, same auth edge you have today**. (Alternatives: GCLB + IAP, or public IP + HTTPS behind CF.) That fully solves the "L4 has no inbound HTTP" problem without exposing the box. For Cloud Run you'd instead need a Service (Jobs can't receive HTTP) or keep the control-plane as a proxy that streams bytes CP→L4 — messier.

**Reliability:** put the video on a **Persistent Disk** (durable, replicated, survives crash/stop) — that's your "reliable disk"; avoid Local SSD (fast but ephemeral). Single-VM risk you cover exactly as you proposed: the **async copy-to-bucket during segmentation** is the durability backstop, and a Managed Instance Group + health check buys HA later if you want it.

**Net recommendation:** if you commit to upload-on-L4, you're committing to an always-available GPU box — and a **GCE L4 VM + persistent SSD + Cloudflare Tunnel** is the natural, likely-cheaper-than-Cloud-Run-always-on fit, with the Cloud Run Job kept as a scale-to-zero burst/fallback path. 

Want me to drop this pricing + the Cloudflare-Tunnel recommendation into **#524** as a comment so the design chip anchors on it?

Sources: [Accelerator-optimized VM pricing](https://cloud.google.com/products/compute/pricing/accelerator-optimized), [g2-standard-8 (CloudPrice)](https://cloudprice.net/gcp/compute/instances/g2-standard-8), [Cloud Run pricing](https://cloud.google.com/run/pricing), [Cloud Run GPUs GA](https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/serverless/cloud-run-gpus-are-now-generally-available), [Cloud Run GPU docs](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/configuring/services/gpu)

|---|---|---|---|

Net: cold-start fully hidden → post-upload wait ≈ validate + detect only. Gotchas I'd bake into the design: (a) a **timeout** so a warm worker exits if no input arrives (abandoned upload), (b) only speculate past a size/`init` threshold so tiny clips don't waste a spin-up, (c) since validation gates dispatch today, run **validation on that same warm L4** (item 1) so one warm box does validate→detect→exit — if the clip fails validation it just exits, no separate CP phase. That's the elegant end state: **spin-up ‖ upload, then validate→detect back-to-back on one warm box, zero cross-machine copies.**

### 217. assistant (2026-07-08T06:03:17.944Z)

**perf/latency, near-term.** Split out of the #524 epic as an independently shippable win. Related: #524 (L4-primary), #521 (compile→L4 + phase placement), #520 (tracing).

## Problem
The L4 worker's cold-start (image pull + torch/CUDA init + GCSFuse mount) sits **in the critical path, serialized AFTER upload + validation**. Measured on the 2026-07-08 live CUJ (report §9): dispatch → `[worker] pulled` = **~55 s**, and it doesn't begin until validation has already passed. So the user pays: upload (~3 min) → CP validation (~183 s) → **then** ~55 s of cold-start → then detect (~161 s). The ~55 s is dead time the user waits through even though nothing about it depends on the upload being finished.

## Idea — speculative "warm-and-wait" dispatch at upload-start
Fire the worker the moment the upload begins (`/api/upload/init`, or the first part / a size threshold), in a **warm-and-wait** mode, so its ~55 s boot overlaps the multi-minute upload and is fully hidden. By the time `/api/upload/complete` lands, the worker is already warm and consumes the input immediately.

### Mechanism (sketch)
- Control-plane, on upload-init (past a threshold), dispatches `rally-worker` in a new mode, e.g. `--await-input=<gs://…/uploads/<upload_id>/<name>>` + `--await-marker=<gs://…/_dispatch/<id>.ready>` + `--await-timeout=<sec>`.
- Worker boots (the ~55 s), then **polls** for the ready marker / input object instead of expecting it to already exist.
- `/api/upload/complete` (or `/api/process`) writes the object + a ready-marker; the warm worker proceeds: **validate (if placed on the L4 — see #524 near-term win) → detect → outputs + done-marker**.
- `/api/process` **adopts** the already-running speculative exec (bind `video_id` at complete); if there is none / it died, it **falls back to today's dispatch-after-validation path** (reuse the #515 exec-status fast-fail to detect a dead speculative exec).

### Design guards (must-haves)
- **Timeout / self-exit**: a warm worker that gets no input within `await_timeout` exits cleanly (abandoned or failed upload) — bounds the wasted GPU.
- **Threshold gate**: only speculate past a size/`init` threshold; tiny/fast uploads don't need it and would just waste a spin-up.
- **Validation on the same warm box**: run validation on the speculative L4 (composes with #524 item 1) so ONE warm box does validate→detect→exit; a clip that fails validation just exits — no separate CP validation phase, no wasted second dispatch.
- **Config-guarded + reversible**: a flag to disable speculation; default = today's dispatch-after-validation behavior. Keep the non-speculative path as the fallback.
- **Cache/dedup interaction**: `video_id` isn't known until upload-complete (the streamed MD5), so speculation keys off `upload_id`/path; if the completed `video_id` turns out already-processed (#448 dedup) or cached-valid (#518), the CP cancels/ignores the speculative exec (best-effort — a rare wasted spin-up).

## Cost
A speculative spin-up wastes at most a partial L4 exec (~$0.1–0.5) when an upload is abandoned or fails — bounded by the timeout + threshold guards. In the common (successful) case it costs nothing extra: the worker would have spun up anyway, just later.

## Acceptance
- With speculation on, cold-start is hidden: on a normal run the worker is warm before `/complete`, so post-upload wait ≈ validate + detect only (no ~55 s gap). Prove with the #520 trace / control-plane+worker timestamps.
- Abandoned/failed upload → the warm worker times out and exits; no orphaned exec, bounded waste.
- Speculation off (default until validated) reproduces today's behavior exactly.
- Full suite + ruff + mypy + leak_scan + check_md_links green; coverage ≥ floor. Mirror `tests/test_api_cloud_dispatch.py` / `tests/test_worker.py`.

## Evidence
`docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_validation-latency_2026-07-07.md` §9 — dispatch `04:32:27` → `[worker] pulled 04:33:20` = ~55 s cold-start, serialized after a 183 s validation. Latency budget + priority order in #524 (comment).

_Filed from the 2026-07-08 live-CUJ latency analysis._

1	# Cloud Build config for the Cloud Run GPU worker image (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M7).
2	#
3	# `gcloud builds submit --tag … -f …` does NOT work — `gcloud builds submit` has no `-f` flag, so a
4	# non-root Dockerfile needs a build CONFIG like this one. Build with:
5	#
6	#   gcloud builds submit --config=deploy/cloudrun/cloudbuild.yaml --project <PROJECT> .
7	#
8	# Override the region/repo/image with --substitutions=_REGION=…,_REPO=…,_IMAGE=… if needed.
9	# PROJECT_ID is provided by Cloud Build automatically.
10	substitutions:
11	  _REGION: asia-southeast1   # L4 GPU is region-limited (asia-southeast1 / europe-west1 / europe-west4 / us-central1 / us-east4)
12	  _REPO: rally
13	  _IMAGE: rally-worker
14	steps:
15	  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
16	    args:
17	      - build
18	      - -t
19	      - '${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/${_REPO}/${_IMAGE}:latest'
20	      - -f
21	      - deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile
22	      - .
23	images:
24	  - '${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${PROJECT_ID}/${_REPO}/${_IMAGE}:latest'
25	options:

### 233. user (2026-07-08T07:15:10.392Z)

1	"""Phase → machine placement resolution (#521).
2	
3	A *phase placement* answers one question per pipeline phase — **where does the heavy compute for
4	this phase run?** — with a config knob (``deployment.phase_placement``) instead of code. The three
5	placeable phases and their values:
6	
7	    validation  →  control_plane | cloud_run_l4
8	    segment     →  control_plane | cloud_run_l4   (a.k.a. "detect")
9	    compile     →  control_plane | cloud_run_l4   (a.k.a. "stitch")
10	
11	``control_plane`` runs the phase IN-PROCESS on the (CPU-only) control-plane service; ``cloud_run_l4``
12	dispatches it to the GPU/NVENC Cloud Run worker Job (the same L4 detection already uses). This module
13	is the SINGLE source of truth so ``backend.orchestration`` (segment + compile) and the worker never
14	re-derive the mapping.
15	
16	**Default = today's behaviour (pure / additive).** When a phase's knob is unset (``None``) the
17	resolver reproduces the pre-#521 placement:
18	
19	    validation  →  control_plane                                   (the authoritative gate, #512/#513)
20	    segment     →  cloud_run_l4 IFF deployment.compute_target == "cloud_run", else control_plane
21	    compile     →  control_plane                                   (the in-process ffmpeg stitch)
22	
23	so a config with no ``phase_placement`` block behaves byte-identically to before this module existed.
24	"""
25	
26	from __future__ import annotations
27	
28	from typing import Any
29	
30	# Phase identities (kept as constants so callers don't stringly-type the phase names).
31	PHASE_VALIDATION = "validation"
32	PHASE_SEGMENT = "segment"
33	PHASE_COMPILE = "compile"
34	PHASES: tuple[str, ...] = (PHASE_VALIDATION, PHASE_SEGMENT, PHASE_COMPILE)
35	
36	# Placement targets.
37	PLACE_CONTROL_PLANE = "control_plane"
38	PLACE_CLOUD_L4 = "cloud_run_l4"
39	PLACEMENTS: tuple[str, ...] = (PLACE_CONTROL_PLANE, PLACE_CLOUD_L4)
40	
41	
42	def resolve_phase_placement(cfg: Any, phase: str) -> str:
43	    """Where does ``phase`` run — ``"control_plane"`` or ``"cloud_run_l4"``?
44	
45	    Precedence: an explicit ``deployment.phase_placement.<phase>`` wins; otherwise the legacy default
46	    (mirrors the pre-#521 behaviour, see the module docstring). Never raises — an absent
47	    ``deployment`` / ``phase_placement`` degrades to the legacy default.
48	    """
49	    deployment = getattr(cfg, "deployment", None)
50	    placement = getattr(deployment, "phase_placement", None)
51	    explicit = getattr(placement, phase, None) if placement is not None else None
52	    if explicit:
53	        return str(explicit)
54	    # Legacy defaults — reproduce today's placement exactly.
55	    if phase == PHASE_SEGMENT:
56	        compute_target = (getattr(deployment, "compute_target", "") or "").strip()
57	        return PLACE_CLOUD_L4 if compute_target == "cloud_run" else PLACE_CONTROL_PLANE
58	    # validation + compile default to the control-plane (their pre-#521 home).
59	    return PLACE_CONTROL_PLANE
60

### 235. user (2026-07-08T07:15:52.536Z)

1	# Phase → machine placement + compile-on-L4 (#521)
2	
3	> **Status:** ✅ **SHIPPED (code)** · **Owner:** Avi · **Created:** 2026-07-08 · **Issue:** [#521](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/521)
4	> **Relation:** extends [COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING](./README.md) (the `ComputeBackend` dispatch seam) and **refines D16(b)** (NVENC-encode-deferred-behind-a-config-knob) now that the 2026-07-08 live CUJ supplies the cost data D16(b) said to revisit on.
5	> **Evidence:** `runlogs/DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_validation-latency_2026-07-07.md` §9 (2026-07-08 live CUJ, rev `rally-control-plane-00019`).
6	
7	---
8	
9	## 0. TL;DR
10	
11	The compile/stitch phase used to run **in-process on the CPU-only 2-vCPU control-plane**, where the
12	2026-07-08 live CUJ measured **~12m55s** for an 11-clip / ~80 s reel from a 1.83 GiB 4K source
13	(~45–90 s per ~7 s clip — the per-clip branding watermark forces a full 4K re-encode with **no
14	NVENC**). Validation (183 s) runs there too; both serialize on `_LOCAL_COMPUTE_LANE`, so one run pins
15	the control-plane for ~16 min → the API can't scale past a handful of concurrent users.
16	
17	#521 makes **where each pipeline phase runs a config edit, not a code change**, and moves the stitch
18	to the **L4 worker (NVENC + 8 vCPU)** the way detection already dispatches:
19	
20	1. **`deployment.phase_placement`** — a per-phase knob (`validation` / `segment` / `compile`) with
21	   values `control_plane | cloud_run_l4`. Unset ⇒ **today's placement, byte-identical**.
22	2. **compile → L4** — a `kind='compile'` Cloud Run dispatch mirroring the detect path: the
23	   control-plane resolves the reel's segments, stages a compile-spec to the bucket, dispatches
24	   `python -m backend.worker compile` on the L4, bucket-polls a done-marker, and returns the same
25	   `{status, filepath, download_url}` shape the SPA already expects.
26	3. **NVENC on the L4** — `deployment.cloud_stitch_encoder` (default `h264_nvenc`) is forwarded onto
27	   the worker's `stitching.encoder`; libx264 stays the everywhere-safe fallback (auto-probed).
28	
29	The cloud-serving preset opts compile onto the L4 (`phase_placement.compile=cloud_run_l4`).
30	
31	---
32	
33	## 1. The placement model
34	
35	`resolve_phase_placement(cfg, phase)` (in `backend/config/placement.py`) is the **single source of
36	truth** — orchestration and the worker never re-derive the mapping. Precedence: an explicit
37	`deployment.phase_placement.<phase>` wins; otherwise the **legacy default** reproduces pre-#521
38	behaviour exactly:
39	
40	| phase | knob unset (legacy default) | meaning |
41	|---|---|---|
42	| `validation` | `control_plane` | the authoritative gate (#512/#513) stays on the control-plane |
43	| `segment` (detect) | `cloud_run_l4` **iff** `compute_target==cloud_run`, else `control_plane` | today's DETECT dispatch |
44	| `compile` (stitch) | `control_plane` | the in-process ffmpeg stitch |
45	
46	So a config with **no** `phase_placement` block behaves exactly as before this existed. To relocate a
47	phase, set its knob:
48	
49	```jsonc
50	// Move the stitch to the L4 (what the cloud-serving preset does):
51	{ "deployment": { "compute_target": "cloud_run",
52	                  "phase_placement": { "compile": "cloud_run_l4" } } }
53	
54	// Force detection back onto the control-plane on a cloud box (e.g. a debugging run):
55	{ "deployment": { "compute_target": "cloud_run",
56	                  "phase_placement": { "segment": "control_plane" } } }
57	
58	// Move validation off the control-plane too (must pair with a cloud segment — see §4):
59	{ "deployment": { "compute_target": "cloud_run",
60	                  "phase_placement": { "validation": "cloud_run_l4" } } }
61	```
62	
63	`control_plane` runs the phase in-process on the (CPU-only) control-plane service; `cloud_run_l4`
64	dispatches it to the GPU/NVENC Cloud Run worker Job.
65	
66	---
67	
68	## 2. compile → L4 dispatch (the flow)
69	
70	`orchestration._execute_compile` consults `resolve_phase_placement(cfg, "compile")`. When it resolves
71	to `cloud_run_l4` **and** cloud dispatch is available (`_cloud_available`), it calls
72	`_dispatch_compile_remote`; otherwise it stitches in-process via the shared `_stitch_reel` core
73	(unchanged).
74	
75	`_dispatch_compile_remote` (mirrors `_maybe_dispatch_remote`):
76	
77	1. **Resolve** the reel's segments HERE — the control-plane owns the DB (`_resolve_compile` →
78	   `(video, kept, out_name)`), applying the `stitching.min_shot_count` floor as before.
79	2. **Stage a compile-spec** to `<output_root>/_compile/<token>/spec.json` — the resolved
80	   `time_pairs`, the control-plane's resolved `stitching` config (watermark + padding, with `encoder`
81	   swapped to `cloud_stitch_encoder`), the source `video_uri`, `video_id`, `output_name`, `locale`.
82	   The worker therefore needs **no control-plane DB** — everything rides the spec.
83	3. **Dispatch** a `compile` Cloud Run Job execution (`CompileJobSpec` → `CloudRunCompute.run_compile`)
84	   running `python -m backend.worker compile --spec-uri … --done-uri … --set storage.output_root=…`,
85	   forwarding the bucket coordinates (the worker Job has no `DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE`).
86	4. **Bucket-poll** the done-marker at `<output_root>/_compile/<token>.done` — the SAME
87	   `_await_marker` machinery `run_infer` uses (crash-fast on a terminal-without-marker execution,
88	   rescue-or-cancel on a poll timeout). Distinct `_compile/` prefix so a compile and a detection for
89	   one bucket never collide on a marker.
90	5. The worker (`cmd_compile`) reads the spec, stitches the reel via the **shared `_stitch_reel`
91	   core** (no forked pipeline, called with `require_mirror=True`), mirror-uploads it to
92	   `<output_root>/highlights/<video_id>/<name>`, and writes the marker (rc + `download_url` +
93	   `reel_uri`). On the worker the mirror is the **only** delivery (its local reel is discarded when
94	   the Job exits), so a mirror-upload failure — or a non-bucket `output_root` — **fails the stitch**
95	   (rc≠0), never a success marker for a reel the SPA can't download. On the control-plane path the
96	   mirror stays best-effort (the CP-local file is a valid fallback).
97	6. The control-plane returns `{status, filepath, download_url, video_id}` — the reel is already in the
98	   bucket, so `/api/highlights` serves it via the existing signed-URL 307. On a worker rc≠0 →
99	   `status='failed'` with the worker's message (clean SPA verdict, never a crash).
100	
101	**Fallback:** if compile is placed on the L4 but the box isn't actually cloud-wired (no output bucket
102	/ Cloud Run env), `_dispatch_compile_remote` logs and stitches in-process — a slow reel beats no reel.
103	
104	---
105	
106	## 3. NVENC on the L4 — and the D5/D16(b) parity note
107	
108	The dominant term in the CUJ stitch was the **per-clip 4K re-encode** the branding watermark forces
109	(~67 s/clip on 2 vCPU with libx264). The L4 has a hardware H.264/HEVC encoder, so #521 adds an encoder
110	knob:
111	
112	- `StitchingConfig.encoder` — `libx264` (default, CPU, works everywhere incl. CI) | `h264_nvenc` |
113	  `hevc_nvenc`. `VideoCompiler` **probes** the requested encoder once per run (a 1-frame lavfi test
114	  encode) and **falls back to libx264** if this ffmpeg build can't run it, so a misconfig / non-GPU
115	  box never breaks. All clips share the one chosen encoder → the concat stays `-c copy`.
116	- `deployment.cloud_stitch_encoder` (default `h264_nvenc`) — what the compile-dispatch forwards onto
117	  the worker's `stitching.encoder` for the L4 run. Set it to `libx264` to run the L4 stitch on CPU.
118	- The control-plane's **own** local stitch always uses `stitching.encoder` (libx264 by default) — the
119	  CP has no GPU.
120	
121	**Parity note (honest):** [README.md](./README.md) **D5/D16(b)** kept stitch **encode = libx264** to
122	protect a byte-exact stitch parity guarantee, and explicitly **deferred NVENC encode behind a config
123	knob, "revisit only if M7 cost data justifies relaxing parity."** The 2026-07-08 CUJ (~13 min stitch,
124	a hard scaling wall past ~5 concurrent users) **is** that cost data. #521 lands exactly the deferred
125	shape — NVENC **behind a config knob**, libx264 the default, config-reversible per phase. Enabling
126	NVENC relaxes byte-identity **at the reel ENCODE layer only** (a cosmetic, shareable artifact); the
127	**WINDOW/rally-level parity** (the analytical output) is untouched — detection and windowing are
128	unchanged. If bit-exact reels are ever required, set `cloud_stitch_encoder=libx264`.
129	
130	> Follow-up (tracked, not in #521's first cut): a **pre-rendered / overlay-filter watermark** so the
131	> stitch isn't a full re-encode at all (the deeper win beyond swapping the encoder).
132	
133	---
134	
135	## 4. Validation placement + the "nobody validates" guard
136	
137	`phase_placement.validation` moves the CPU-bound validator suite off the 2-vCPU control-plane too,
138	via the existing `--skip-validation` lever (#513):
139	
140	- **`control_plane`** (default): the control-plane runs the validators and is the authoritative gate;
141	  the worker **skips** its redundant re-validation (`skip_validation=True`) — today's #512/#513
142	  behaviour, byte-identical.
143	- **`cloud_run_l4`**: when detection **actually** dispatches to the L4, the control-plane **delegates**
144	  — it does NOT run the validator suite (`_execute_processing` skips it), and the dispatch carries
145	  **no** `--skip-validation`, so the **worker** validates and rejects a bad clip via a non-zero rc
146	  (surfaced as a failed job).
147	
148	**Footgun guard:** a clip can only be validated on the worker if the worker also **detects** it there.
149	Two protections:
150	
151	1. **Config-load guard** (`DeploymentConfig` model-validator): `validation=cloud_run_l4` requires the
152	   effective segment placement to also be `cloud_run_l4` (explicit, or via `compute_target=cloud_run`)
153	   — else config load fails with an actionable error.
154	2. **Runtime guard** (`_will_detect_on_cloud`): if a per-request `compute='local'` forces detection
155	   in-process (no worker), the control-plane **still validates** — never leave a clip unvalidated
156	   because nobody ran the gate.
157	
158	---
159	
160	## 5. Files & tests
161	
162	| Area | Code | Tests |
163	|---|---|---|
164	| Placement resolver + config | `backend/config/placement.py`, `backend/config/models.py` (`PhasePlacementConfig`, `DeploymentConfig.phase_placement` / `cloud_stitch_encoder`, guard) | `tests/test_phase_placement.py`, `tests/test_config_deployment.py` |
165	| Preset | `backend/config/presets.py` (`cloud-serving` → `phase_placement.compile=cloud_run_l4`) | `tests/test_phase_placement.py` |
166	| compile dispatch | `backend/orchestration.py` (`_execute_compile`, `_dispatch_compile_remote`, `_stitch_reel`, `_will_detect_on_cloud`) | `tests/test_compile_dispatch.py` |
167	| compute backend | `backend/compute/base.py` (`CompileJobSpec`, `run_compile`), `backend/compute/cloud_run.py` (`run_compile`, `_await_marker`) | `tests/test_compute_backend.py` |
168	| worker | `backend/worker/__main__.py` (`compile` subcommand, `cmd_compile`) | `tests/test_worker.py` |
169	| NVENC encoder | `backend/pipeline/stitcher/compiler.py` (`encoder`, `_select_encoder`, `_probe_encoder_available`) | `tests/test_compiler.py` |
170	| validation delegation | `backend/orchestration.py` (`_maybe_dispatch_remote` skip flag, `_execute_processing`) | `tests/test_api_cloud_dispatch.py` |
171	
172	All GPU-free + cloud-free: a fake `CloudRunJobClient` simulates the worker (reads the staged spec,
173	writes the reel + done-marker into a local "bucket"), mirroring `tests/test_api_cloud_dispatch.py`.
174

### 244. assistant (2026-07-08T07:19:25.103Z)

**process / infra.** Motivated by the 2026-07-08 release (deploying #525/#527/#528/#530 to prod). Related: #521 (the `phase_placement` config layer — the first citizen of the feature-flag layer), #524, #526.

## Problem — binary releases and feature rollouts are coupled, and "what's live" is ambiguous
1. **Both surfaces ride the mutable `:latest` tag.** `rally-control-plane` (service) and `rally-worker` (Job) are both deployed as `…/rally-worker:latest`. So:
   - "What image is live?" needs a digest lookup, and **rollback relies on remembering a digest** — this release had to *hunt down* the prior digest (`sha256:c6393d27…`) by hand to establish a rollback anchor. A revision also silently rolled `00018→00019` between sessions unnoticed for the same reason.
   - `:latest` is not reproducible: two builds of the same source get different digests, and a stray `builds submit` can move the tag under a running service.
2. **Feature flags are baked into the image.** The wins (`phase_placement.compile=cloud_run_l4`, `cloud_stitch_encoder=h264_nvenc`, `trust_submit_video_id`, validation placement) live in the `cloud-serving` **preset** (`backend/config/presets.py`) — compiled into the binary. **Rolling a feature out or back therefore requires a full rebuild + redeploy.** There's no way to flip a flag independently of shipping a binary, no canary, no per-environment override that isn't ad-hoc env vars (the drift trap flagged in #524).
3. **No documented runbook or release record.** Deploy knowledge is scattered across the `DEPLOY_REPORT_*` runlogs; there's no single "build → deploy → smoke → rollback" runbook, and no manifest of {which digest, which revision, which flags} is live.

## Ask
### A. Release process (binary)
- **Versioned, immutable tags**: tag every build with the **git SHA** (and optionally a semver), e.g. `rally-worker:<sha>`; keep `:latest` only as a convenience pointer. Update `deploy/cloudrun/cloudbuild.yaml` to tag `:${SHORT_SHA}`.
- **Deploy by digest, never by `:latest`** — resolve `:<sha>`→digest and deploy the pinned digest to both surfaces (reproducible; rollback = redeploy the prior pinned digest).
- **Runbook** in `deploy/cloudrun/README.md`: build → deploy worker Job → deploy CP (new revision) → post-deploy smoke (`post_deploy_smoke.ps1`) → verify health → **one-command rollback** (`update-traffic --to-revisions <prev>=100` for the CP; `jobs update --image <prev-digest>` for the worker).
- **Live-state manifest**: a single source of truth (a checked-in `deploy/cloudrun/RELEASES.md` or a labels convention) recording the live digest + revision per surface, updated each release.

### B. Config / feature-rollout layer (decoupled from the binary)
- **Runtime-togglable flags** for the placement/encoder/validation knobs so a feature can be rolled out/back **without a rebuild** — e.g. a per-environment config object (env-group / Secret Manager / a small remote-config doc in GCS the CP reads on boot or on a signal), layered OVER the baked preset defaults with clear precedence.
- **Canary / gradual rollout**: ability to enable a flag for a slice (a % of traffic via Cloud Run revisions, or per-user) before 100%.
- **Drift-proof**: the live flag state is observable and matches a checked-in source of truth (ties to #520's tracing — emit the resolved config per run).
- `phase_placement` (#521) is the natural first citizen: today it's preset-baked; it should be an env/remote-config override so `compile→L4` or `validation→L4` can be flipped per-environment without shipping a binary.

## Acceptance
- A build produces an immutable `:<sha>`-tagged image; both surfaces deploy by digest; rollback is one documented command.
- The placement/encoder/validation flags can be toggled at deploy/runtime without rebuilding the image.
- A runbook + live-state manifest exist and are updated by the release flow.

## Evidence
2026-07-08 release of #525/#527/#528/#530: both surfaces on `:latest`; rollback digest (`c6393d27`) had to be recovered by hand; the wins are preset-baked so this release couldn't toggle them without the rebuild.

_Filed at owner request from the 2026-07-08 release._
