### 248. user (2026-07-08T07:19:43.637Z)

1	# Cloud Run GPU serving — build / deploy / run (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M6/M7)
2	
3	The cloud-serving compute path: a control plane (`CloudRunCompute`, `backend/compute/cloud_run.py`)
4	dispatches a Cloud Run **Job** execution running this image's worker on an **L4**, then **bucket-polls**
5	a done-marker for completion (D16). Scale-to-zero (D7) — no warm pool.
6	
7	> **Status:** the dispatch shim + image are **landed + mock-tested** (M6). The **real build + push +
8	> run on an L4** is the **M7** owner step, within the **$100/₹10k** budget (D17) — confirm cmd + hourly
9	> cost first, **delete every resource after** ([[gcp-vm-always-delete]]). CI does **not** build this image.
10	
11	## 0. Prerequisites (owner)
12	- A GCP project with billing on + the Cloud Run, Artifact Registry, and Cloud Build APIs enabled.
13	- GPU quota for L4 (`GPUS_ALL_REGIONS` ≥ 1) in the chosen region (asia-south1, else Singapore — see
14	  `deploy/gcp/README.md`; L4 is often stocked-out, fall back per that runbook).
15	- The WASB weights staged in the bucket (WASB-C3 unresolved → owner-gated): `gs://<bucket>/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar`.
16	
17	## 1. Build + push the image (Artifact Registry)
18	```bash
19	PROJECT=<gcp-project>; REPO=rally
20	# L4 GPU is region-limited — use asia-southeast1 (Singapore), NOT asia-south1 (Mumbai).
21	REGION=asia-southeast1
22	gcloud artifacts repositories create $REPO --repository-format=docker --location=$REGION 2>/dev/null || true
23	# `gcloud builds submit` has NO -f flag, so a non-root Dockerfile needs a build config:
24	gcloud builds submit --config=deploy/cloudrun/cloudbuild.yaml --project $PROJECT .
25	```
26	*(A CUDA + micromamba image is large; the first build is ~8–10 min. Budget-watch per D17.)*
27	
28	## 2. Create the Cloud Run **Job** (GPU, scale-to-zero)
29	The proven shape (first real M7 deploy, 2026-07-03): weights staged in a **same-region bucket**
30	mounted read-only via GCS FUSE (no weights in the image — WASB-C3; no init-fetch step needed).
31	```bash
32	gcloud run jobs create rally-worker \
33	  --image $REGION-docker.pkg.dev/$PROJECT/$REPO/$IMG:latest \
34	  --region $REGION --gpu 1 --gpu-type nvidia-l4 --no-gpu-zonal-redundancy \
35	  --cpu 8 --memory 32Gi \
36	  --max-retries 0 --task-timeout 3600 \
37	  --set-env-vars WASB_WEIGHTS=/gcs/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar \
38	  --add-volume name=serving,type=cloud-storage,bucket=$SERVING_BUCKET \
39	  --add-volume-mount volume=serving,mount-path=/gcs
40	```
41	- **`--no-gpu-zonal-redundancy` is REQUIRED on the CLI**, not just a cost choice (it also halves
42	  the GPU rate — right for batch jobs): without the flag `gcloud` asks an interactive Y/n which,
43	  in a non-interactive shell, **hangs forever with zero output and nothing created**. An empty
44	  `gcloud` that "did nothing" ⇒ suspect a hidden prompt.
45	- 8 vCPU / 32 GiB: x264 encode is CPU-side, so CPU headroom keeps the paid GPU busy. `/tmp` is
46	  **RAM-backed** — the pulled input video lives in memory, so size `--memory` for the largest
47	  expected upload (a 5.8 GB GoPro file ≈ 6 GiB of tmpfs).
48	
49	## 2a. GPU-resident WASB decode (#506/#507) — the current serving default
50	The image ships the **py3.10 + torch 2.6.0+cu124 + PyNvVideoCodec==2.1.0** WASB env with
51	`libnvcuvid`/`libnvidia-encode` **baked in** (multi-stage layer from the matching NVIDIA driver —
52	Cloud Run injects the host driver, not these userspace codec libs). `indexing.wasb.decode_backend`
53	selects the decode path:
54	- `cpu` (model default) — the historical cv2 decode; **byte-unchanged** by this work.
55	- `nvdec_resident` — NVDEC decode + cv2-faithful affine `grid_sample` resize + GPU normalize, all
56	  on-GPU. **The cloud-serving preset default** (validated: offline golden gate @ SERVED F1 0.574 ≈
57	  baseline 0.582, AND on the live L4 the served GX010128 trajectory reproduced the VM run —
58	  26 479 ≈ 26 481 visible). CUDA-only (config-validator-enforced).
59	
60	**How the knob reaches the worker.** The worker Job runs with NO `DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE`, so it does
61	**not** apply the cloud-serving preset itself. The control plane (which *does* have the profile)
62	resolves the config and **forwards** `indexing.wasb.{decode_backend,batch_size,prefetch_batches}`
63	to the worker as `--set` overrides (`orchestration.py`, alongside `skip_ai_handoff`). So the
64	**preset is the single source of truth** — changing `presets.py` + redeploying the control plane
65	changes serving; no per-Job knob needed.
66	
67	**Override / rollback lever (no redeploy).** The native runner reads `WASB_DECODE_BACKEND` FIRST,
68	so a Job env var wins over the forwarded `--set`:
69	```bash
70	# force nvdec (or pin cpu) on the Job regardless of the forwarded config; instant rollback = remove it
71	gcloud run jobs update rally-worker --region $REGION --update-env-vars WASB_DECODE_BACKEND=nvdec_resident
72	gcloud run jobs update rally-worker --region $REGION --remove-env-vars WASB_DECODE_BACKEND   # → forwarded/preset default
73	# full revert: pin the prior image by digest
74	gcloud run jobs update rally-worker --region $REGION --image $REGION-docker.pkg.dev/$PROJECT/$REPO/rally-worker@sha256:<prev>
75	```
76	⚠ Forwarding `decode_backend=nvdec_resident` requires a worker image that understands
77	`--decode-backend` (≥ #507). Roll the **worker image and the control plane together** — a new
78	control plane forwarding nvdec to an old worker image argparse-fails (rc 2).
79	
80	⚠ **Same coupling for `--skip-validation` (worker skips redundant re-validation).** The control
81	plane is the authoritative validation gate — it runs the validators with its resolved config and
82	only dispatches on PASS — so it now passes a **`--skip-validation`** flag on **every** dispatch,
83	telling the worker not to re-decode + re-validate (which used its own default thresholds and could
84	reject a clip the gate already passed). This flag is a **new worker CLI arg**: an OLDER worker image
85	that predates it **argparse-aborts (rc 2) BEFORE writing the done-marker**, so — unlike the `--set`
86	knobs, which an old worker at least parses — the dispatcher's bucket-poll gets no marker and waits
87	the **full `deployment.remote_poll_max_wait_sec` (~65 min default)** before failing opaquely as
88	`cloud dispatch error`, on *every* dispatch. So again: **roll the worker image and the control plane
89	together, worker-first.** Reverse skew (new worker + old control plane) is safe — the old control
90	plane simply never sends the flag, and the new worker validates by default.
91	
92	**Post-deploy smoke.** After a cutover, confirm the real L4 path with
93	`deploy/cloudrun/post_deploy_smoke.ps1` (run from PowerShell — see §Gotchas on MSYS arg-mangling):
94	```powershell
95	./deploy/cloudrun/post_deploy_smoke.ps1 -Video gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/mahadevpura_smoke_180s.mp4 -Decode cpu    # image sanity (fast)
96	./deploy/cloudrun/post_deploy_smoke.ps1 -Video gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/GX010128.MP4       -Decode env    # nvdec via preset/env (hard clip)
97	```
98	
99	## 3. Wire the dispatcher (control plane)
100	The control plane runs with the **cloud-serving** profile (`DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving` →
101	`compute_target=cloud_run`). `get_compute_backend(config)` then builds `CloudRunCompute` from env:
102	```bash
103	export DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving
104	export CLOUD_RUN_JOB=rally-worker  CLOUD_RUN_REGION=$REGION  GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=$PROJECT
105	# A `worker infer` (or the API job path) with a gs:// video now DISPATCHES to the Job instead of
106	# running in-process; the container runs the SAME worker INLINE (compute_target forced local_gpu).
107	python -m backend.worker infer --video-uri gs://<bucket>/videos/clip.mp4 --out-uri gs://<bucket> --segmenter wasb_hybrid
108	```
109	**The AI handoff needs its key IN THE JOB env** (`GEMINI_API_KEY` — mount a Secret Manager secret
110	on the job), or run fully-local with `--set indexing.skip_ai_handoff=true` (candidate windows
111	emitted as rallies — the eval-parity mode). With neither, the segmenter used to bail to a silent
112	0-rally "success" **after** the paid GPU pass; the cloud-serving startup checks now fail this at
113	boot (`AI_HANDOFF_KEY_MISSING`, rc 2) before the video is even pulled.
114	
115	### 3a. Control-plane SERVICE deploy — required flags for reliable async jobs
116	
117	The API server (`/api/process`, `/api/compile` — #329) runs its **job worker IN-PROCESS**: the
118	request returns `202` and a background thread drains the job (GPU detection dispatches to the Job;
119	the reel stitch runs on the control plane itself). On Cloud Run that background thread only makes
120	progress while the instance has CPU, so a scale-to-zero **service** deploy MUST set:
121	
122	**The canonical deploy path is the committed manifest generator** (#473) — it bakes the
123	cloud-serving config.json into a startup wrapper, sets every flag below, pins `maxScale=1`
124	(per-instance SQLite state, issue #471), and deliberately does NOT set `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` (a
125	reserved runtime env name that gets a manifest rejected; the code default is already right):
126	```bash
127	python deploy/cloudrun/gen_service_yaml.py --edge-auth on -o service.yaml
128	gcloud run services replace service.yaml --region $REGION   # from PowerShell on Windows
129	```
130	`--edge-auth off` is only for the pre-CF identity-token smoke/e2e window; redeploy with `on`
131	before testers touch the service again. The flag-based equivalent (for reference — a manifest
132	round-trips the bash wrapper's metacharacters, flags don't on Windows):
133	```bash
134	gcloud run deploy rally-control-plane --image <image> --region $REGION \
135	  --no-cpu-throttling \        # CPU stays allocated between requests → the drain thread finishes
136	  --min-instances 1 \          # one always-warm instance drains reliably (cheap CPU; NOT the D7 GPU pool)
137	  --cpu 2 --memory 4Gi --port 8080 --allow-unauthenticated \
138	  --set-env-vars DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving,CLOUD_RUN_REGION=$REGION,GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=$PROJECT
139	```
140	Without `--no-cpu-throttling`, a `/api/compile` returns 202 and then **stalls** — the reel never
141	finishes and startup crash-recovery would mark it terminal. `min-instances 1` does NOT contradict
142	D7 (that was the ~$500/mo warm **GPU** pool); this is a ~1-vCPU CPU instance. Defense-in-depth is in
143	the code: `Database.recover_stale_jobs()` **re-queues** a compile interrupted by a restart (idempotent
144	stitch) rather than failing it, so even an unlucky scale-down mid-stitch self-heals on the next drain.
145	Reserve `--allow-unauthenticated` for the pre-CF-auth smoke test only; lock ingress to the CF proxy
146	(M9) before inviting testers. The image ships the `auth` extra (PyJWT[crypto]), so flipping
147	`security.edge_auth_enabled` on needs no derived image — the M9 Cf-Access JWT verify works out of
148	the box.
149	
150	## 4. M7 acceptance (the owner runlog)
151	Capture a `DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_<date>.md` under
152	`docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/` (mirrors the truly-local template): the exact commands,
153	rally counts, `gpu_active_seconds`/`wall_seconds`/`bytes_out`, a reel smoke-test (`ffmpeg -f null -`
154	exit 0 + a 5-frame contact sheet), gotchas, and the **$0 teardown audit**.
155	
156	## Gotchas
157	- The dispatched container **must run inline** — `CloudRunCompute` forces `compute_target=local_gpu`
158	  + `indexing.detector_impl=native` in the per-execution args so the Job never re-dispatches itself.
159	- Completion is a **bucket marker** (`<out_uri>/_dispatch/<token>.done`), not the Cloud Run API status
160	  — so it reuses the storage layer + `execution_policy.poll_until` and survives a control-plane restart.
161	- A **failed** Job (validation rc 2 / segmenter rc 3) now writes a **failure marker** carrying the
162	  real non-zero rc, so the dispatcher's poll terminates **FAST + accurately** (no 30-min hang on a bad
163	  video). Only a **hung/crashed** container (no marker at all) makes the bounded poll time out
164	  (`ExecutionPolicy.max_wait_sec`); the worker CLI maps that to a clean **rc 4**, and the API path
165	  surfaces it as a `cloud dispatch error` job failure (not a traceback).
166	- libx264 encode (parity, D16) + NVDEC decode-only; the image keeps ffmpeg's default libx264.
167	- **`GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` is required** for the real client — `GoogleCloudRunJobClient.run_job`
168	  fails fast with a clear `RuntimeError` if it is unset/empty (it must resolve a real `job_path`;
169	  an unset project would otherwise build a bogus `projects/None/...` name). Export it (step 3) before
170	  dispatching. The guard runs before the `google-cloud-run` import, so it is exercised in CI even
171	  though the SDK isn't a CI/test dependency.
172	- **Hand-rolled `jobs execute --args` with `gs://` URIs:** gcloud's default comma-split plus its
173	  alternate-delimiter syntax both bite — repeated `--set` tokens trip the comma parser, and a
174	  `^:^` delimiter splits the `:` inside `gs://`. Use a delimiter that appears nowhere in the args,
175	  e.g. `--args='^@@^-v@@infer@@--video-uri=gs://…'`. (The dispatcher path doesn't have this
176	  problem — prefer it; this is for one-off diagnostics.) Container-arg overrides must include the
177	  dispatcher's two forced `--set`s: `deployment.compute_target=local_gpu`,
178	  `indexing.detector_impl=native`.
179	- **Git Bash (MSYS) on Windows mangles Unix-looking values** before gcloud sees them:
180	  `--add-volume-mount …mount-path=/gcs` became `C:/Program Files/Git/gcs`, and
181	  `--command=/bin/bash` became `C:/Program Files/Git/usr/bin/bash` (the container then exec-fails).
182	  Run gcloud commands with such flags from PowerShell (`MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` breaks gcloud's own
183	  wrapper). PowerShell has its own trap: quote any flag value containing commas.
184	- **First execution after an image push** spends minutes in "Container image import in progress"
185	  (visible in `executions describe status.conditions`, NOT in logs) — that's progress, not a hang;
186	  later executions reuse the imported image.
187

1	"""Generate the control-plane Cloud Run *service manifest* (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8/M9, #473).
2	
3	The A1 alpha deploy drove `rally-control-plane` from a hand-generated YAML that lived in a
4	session scratchpad — this commits the generator so the deploy is reproducible from a clean
5	clone. It emits a Knative service manifest for::
6	
7	    python deploy/cloudrun/gen_service_yaml.py --edge-auth off -o service.yaml
8	    gcloud run services replace service.yaml --region asia-southeast1
9	
10	Why a MANIFEST (`services replace`) and not `gcloud run deploy` flags: the container command is
11	a /bin/bash wrapper carrying shell metacharacters (pipes, redirects, &&) — on Windows the gcloud
12	cmd-shim mangles those through `--command/--args`, while a YAML round-trips them verbatim. Run
13	gcloud from PowerShell, not git-bash (MSYS rewrites `/tmp`-style paths).
14	
15	The app is configured through a config.json baked INTO the manifest: the wrapper base64-decodes
16	it to `/tmp/apphome/config.json` and exports `RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/apphome` before exec'ing the
17	server, so one image serves every config shape (config.json wins over the cloud-serving preset —
18	backend/config/loader.py). `--edge-auth` is REQUIRED and explicit: `off` is only for the
19	identity-token smoke/e2e window; redeploy with `on` (the Cf-Access JWT verify, M9) before the
20	service faces testers again.
21	
22	Deliberate manifest choices (learned on the A1 deploy):
23	  * `run.googleapis.com/cpu-throttling: "false"` + minScale 1 — the in-process job worker drains
24	    on a background thread; without always-allocated CPU a 202'd compile stalls (README §3a).
25	  * maxScale DEFAULTS TO 1 — the jobs/videos DB is per-instance ephemeral SQLite, so a second
26	    instance splits the queue and `/api/jobs/{id}` polls (issue #471, option 1). Raise it only
27	    once state is shared.
28	  * `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is NOT set — it's a reserved runtime env name and the manifest is rejected
29	    with it; the code default ("rally-worker") is already correct.
30	  * No secrets here: bucket/project/aud/team-domain are the same non-secret identifiers the
31	    committed runlogs carry; the Gemini key stays in Secret Manager (the alpha runs
32	    `skip_ai_handoff=true` and never needs it).
33	
34	Baked config pins (CONTROL_PLANE_DURABLE_REBUILD P1/D5 — the live rev-00011 posture, learned on
35	the 2026-07-05 owner CUJ; before this, regenerating the manifest silently DROPPED both):
36	  * `indexing.default_segmenter=wasb_hybrid` — the engine default is yolo_hybrid, so an unpinned
37	    cloud job ran the wrong segmenter (#479).
38	  * `security.max_part_mb=24` — upload parts must fit under the ~32 MiB HTTP/1 body cap at the
39	    Google Front End (#480); the engine default (95) gets every part 413'd before FastAPI runs.
40	    NOT fixed via `use-http2`: h2c requires the container itself to speak cleartext HTTP/2,
41	    which uvicorn does not (spec D6) — the annotation would break the service.
42	  * `--memory` defaults to 8Gi (spec D2) — compile still pulls the gs:// source into the
43	    RAM-backed /tmp to stitch, so the instance must hold the largest allowed upload with
44	    headroom; >8Gi would force 4 vCPU on Cloud Run for no current need.
45	"""
46	
47	from __future__ import annotations
48	
49	import argparse
50	import base64
51	import json
52	import sys
53	
54	DEFAULT_PROJECT = "gen-lang-client-0306026826"
55	DEFAULT_REGION = "asia-southeast1"
56	DEFAULT_SERVICE = "rally-control-plane"
57	DEFAULT_SERVING_BUCKET = "gs://khelsutra-rally-serving"
58	DEFAULT_CF_TEAM_DOMAIN = "https://khelsutra.cloudflareaccess.com"
59	DEFAULT_CF_ACCESS_AUD = "c980da67ed888fff935fcd913ae1eb4da63f9d65805d6e1c5ca76f92f517082b"
60	
61	
62	def build_config(args: argparse.Namespace) -> dict:
63	    """The app config the wrapper materializes at RALLY_APP_HOME/config.json."""
64	    bucket = args.serving_bucket.rstrip("/")
65	    return {
66	        "deployment": {"profile": "cloud-serving", "compute_target": "cloud_run"},
67	        "indexing": {
68	            # WASB-only alpha: candidate windows ARE the rallies; no Gemini key in this service.
69	            "skip_ai_handoff": True,
70	            # rev-00011 pin (#479): the engine default is yolo_hybrid — pin the validated one.
71	            "default_segmenter": args.default_segmenter,
72	        },
73	        "storage": {
74	            "backend": "gcs",
75	            "output_root": bucket,
76	            "input_backend": "gcs",
77	            "input_root": f"{bucket}/videos",
78	        },
79	        "security": {
80	            "edge_auth_enabled": args.edge_auth == "on",
81	            "cf_team_domain": args.cf_team_domain,
82	            "cf_access_aud": args.cf_access_aud,
83	            "allowed_video_root": "/tmp/apphome/output",
84	            # rev-00011 pin (#480): parts must clear the ~32 MiB HTTP/1 cap at the GFE.
85	            "max_part_mb": args.max_part_mb,
86	        },
87	    }
88	
89	
90	def build_yaml(args: argparse.Namespace) -> str:
91	    """Render the Knative service manifest for `gcloud run services replace`."""
92	    cfg_b64 = base64.b64encode(
93	        json.dumps(build_config(args), separators=(",", ":")).encode("utf-8")
94	    ).decode("ascii")
95	    # mkdir BEFORE the redirect (the redirect can't create the directory), then exec so the
96	    # server is PID 1 and Cloud Run's SIGTERM reaches it directly.
97	    wrapper = (
98	        "mkdir -p /tmp/apphome && "
99	        f"printf %s '{cfg_b64}' | base64 -d > /tmp/apphome/config.json && "
100	        "export RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/apphome && "
101	        "exec python3 -m backend.main --server --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8080"
102	    )
103	    return f"""\
104	apiVersion: serving.knative.dev/v1
105	kind: Service
106	metadata:
107	  name: {args.service}
108	  labels:
109	    cloud.googleapis.com/location: {args.region}
110	spec:
111	  template:
112	    metadata:
113	      annotations:
114	        autoscaling.knative.dev/minScale: "{args.min_instances}"
115	        autoscaling.knative.dev/maxScale: "{args.max_instances}"
116	        run.googleapis.com/cpu-throttling: "false"
117	    spec:
118	      timeoutSeconds: {args.timeout}
119	      containers:
120	        - image: {args.image}
121	          command: ["/bin/bash"]
122	          args: ["-c", "{wrapper}"]
123	          ports:
124	            - containerPort: 8080
125	          resources:
126	            limits:
127	              cpu: "{args.cpu}"
128	              memory: {args.memory}
129	          env:
130	            - name: DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE
131	              value: cloud-serving
132	            - name: CLOUD_RUN_REGION
133	              value: {args.region}
134	            - name: GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT
135	              value: {args.project}
136	            - name: RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS
137	              value: "*"
138	"""
139	
140	
141	def main(argv: "list[str] | None" = None) -> int:
142	    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
143	    p.add_argument(
144	        "--edge-auth",
145	        choices=("on", "off"),
146	        required=True,
147	        help="REQUIRED and explicit: 'off' only for the identity-token smoke/e2e window; "
148	        "redeploy 'on' (M9 Cf-Access verify) before testers touch it again.",
149	    )
150	    p.add_argument("--project", default=DEFAULT_PROJECT)
151	    p.add_argument("--region", default=DEFAULT_REGION)
152	    p.add_argument("--service", default=DEFAULT_SERVICE)
153	    p.add_argument(
154	        "--image",
155	        default=None,
156	        help="Container image; default = the project's rally-worker:latest (post-#470 it "
157	        "carries cloud-run + auth, so the worker image IS the control-plane image).",
158	    )
159	    p.add_argument("--serving-bucket", default=DEFAULT_SERVING_BUCKET)
160	    p.add_argument("--cf-team-domain", default=DEFAULT_CF_TEAM_DOMAIN)
161	    p.add_argument("--cf-access-aud", default=DEFAULT_CF_ACCESS_AUD)
162	    p.add_argument(
163	        "--default-segmenter",
164	        default="wasb_hybrid",
165	        help="rev-00011 pin (#479): the engine default is yolo_hybrid; the alpha serves the "
166	        "validated wasb_hybrid.",
167	    )
168	    p.add_argument(
169	        "--max-part-mb",
170	        type=int,
171	        default=24,
172	        help="rev-00011 pin (#480): upload parts must fit under Cloud Run's ~32 MiB HTTP/1 "
173	        "request-body cap (the engine default 95 gets 413'd at the edge).",
174	    )
175	    p.add_argument("--min-instances", type=int, default=1)
176	    p.add_argument(
177	        "--max-instances",
178	        type=int,
179	        default=1,
180	        help="Default 1: per-instance SQLite state splits across instances (issue #471).",
181	    )
182	    p.add_argument("--cpu", type=int, default=2)
183	    p.add_argument(
184	        "--memory",
185	        default="8Gi",
186	        help="Default 8Gi (CONTROL_PLANE_DURABLE_REBUILD D2): compile pulls the gs:// source "
187	        "into RAM-backed /tmp, so size for the largest allowed upload plus headroom.",
188	    )
189	    p.add_argument("--timeout", type=int, default=300)
190	    p.add_argument("-o", "--output", default="-", help="Output path, or '-' for stdout.")
191	    args = p.parse_args(argv)
192	    if args.image is None:
193	        args.image = f"{args.region}-docker.pkg.dev/{args.project}/rally/rally-worker:latest"
194	
195	    text = build_yaml(args)
196	    if args.output == "-":
197	        sys.stdout.write(text)
198	    else:
199	        with open(args.output, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
200	            f.write(text)
201	    return 0
202	
203	
204	if __name__ == "__main__":
205	    raise SystemExit(main())
206

### 298. assistant (2026-07-08T08:04:23.070Z)

Checked the live logs — and the honest answer is **no, there's no percentage available**, for a real reason worth seeing:

The detector runs as **one opaque GPU pass**. The worker logs exactly two lines around it:
```
08:01:51  Running native WASB inference (device=cuda) on in.mp4 ...
   …(nothing)…
(later)   Phase 2 (WASB) completed in <N>s. Candidate windows: <k>
```
No per-frame, per-batch, or `%` output in between — the native WASB runner decodes + infers + builds the trajectory in a single call and only reports at completion. The job status the SPA polls tracks **coarse stages** (`validating → segmenting`), not a percentage. **This is exactly the gap #520 was filed for** — there is no step-level/progress signal to surface today.

Two things I *can* give you:

1. **A throughput ETA** (not a real %): detection started `08:01:51`; on the prior CUJ WASB took ~162 s for a comparable 4K clip, so this should finish roughly **~08:04–08:05** (±, depending on GX010151's length). Let me pull the clip size to tighten that and check if it's already done.

### 306. assistant (2026-07-08T08:09:40.907Z)

Got real numbers for the worker during this 5.8 GiB detection (Monitoring has a ~1–2 min lag; these run to `08:08:59Z`):

| Resource | Utilization | Of allocated |
|---|---|---|
| **GPU (L4)** | **not observable** — metric 404s, worker doesn't self-report | — |
| **CPU** | early ~25% → steady **~14–15%** (~1.1 of 8 vCPU) | 8 vCPU |
| **RAM** | climbed to **~21%** and holding (~6.7 GiB) | 32 GiB |

**What this tells us:**
- **The worker is GPU-bound, with big CPU/RAM headroom.** CPU sits at ~15% because WASB inference runs on the L4 — the CPU is just feeding the GPU. The GPU is doing the real work and is almost certainly the bottleneck, but **we can't see its % — there's no telemetry** (the standard Cloud Run GPU metric doesn't exist for Jobs, and the container doesn't run `nvidia-smi`/DCGM).
- **RAM ~6.7 GiB = the 5.81 GiB clip in RAM-backed `/tmp` + the model.** Comfortable inside 32 GiB — no memory pressure. (This is exactly why the runbook sizes 32 GiB for ~6 GB uploads.)
- The steady readings confirm detection is still churning (7+ min in on a 5.8 GiB clip — consistent with the size-scaled ETA).

So two hard observability gaps are now confirmed on this run: **no detection progress %** and **no GPU utilization**. Both are the same root cause as #520. 

Want me to fold both into #520 as explicit acceptance criteria — (a) a live detector progress heartbeat (`processed N/total`) surfaced to the SPA, and (b) GPU/CPU/RAM self-reporting from the worker (an `nvidia-smi`/DCGM sample logged per interval)? That'd turn "I can't tell you the %" into a first-class dashboard. The lifecycle watcher's still running for the detection-complete + compile→L4 numbers.

### 309. assistant (2026-07-08T08:10:22.186Z)

**bug / observability — for triage.** Concrete runtime-visibility gaps confirmed live on the 2026-07-08 CUJ (rev `rally-control-plane-00020-dtg`, clip GX010151.MP4 = **5.81 GiB**). Companion / sub-slice of #520 (step-level tracing).

## Symptom
During a real run you cannot answer two basic questions:
1. **"How far along is the video?"** — there is **no progress %**. The detector runs as one opaque GPU pass: the worker logs `Running native WASB inference (device=cuda) on in.mp4 …` and then **nothing** until `Phase 2 (WASB) completed in <N>s. Candidate windows: <k>`. No per-frame / per-batch / `%` output in between. The job status the SPA polls only tracks coarse **stages** (`validating → segmenting`), not progress. On this run detection churned **7+ minutes** on the 5.8 GiB clip with zero progress signal.
2. **"Is the GPU actually busy / what's the bottleneck?"** — there is **no GPU telemetry**. `run.googleapis.com/container/gpu/utilization` returns **404 NOT_FOUND** (Cloud Run doesn't emit per-container GPU metrics for Jobs), and the worker container does **not** self-report `nvidia-smi`/DCGM. CPU + RAM *are* in Cloud Monitoring but with ~1–2 min lag and aren't surfaced anywhere user-facing.

## Evidence (this run)
Worker (`rally-worker-956vm`, 8 vCPU / 32 GiB / L4) during detection, via the Monitoring API:
- **CPU:** early ~25% → steady **~14–15% of 8 vCPU** (~1.1 vCPU) — low, because WASB is GPU-bound (CPU just feeds the GPU).
- **RAM:** **~21% of 32 GiB (~6.7 GiB)** — the 5.81 GiB clip in RAM-backed `/tmp` + the model; comfortable.
- **GPU:** **unknown** (metric 404, no self-report) — yet it's the actual bottleneck we most want to see.

So the box that's doing the work (the GPU) is the one thing we're blind to, and the user gets no progress feedback on a multi-minute run.

## Ask (acceptance)
1. **Live detector progress heartbeat** — the WASB runner emits a periodic `processed N/total frames` (or %) at an interval, surfaced through the job status so the SPA can show a real progress bar instead of a spinner.
2. **Worker resource self-report** — the worker samples GPU/CPU/RAM (`nvidia-smi`/DCGM + `/proc`) on an interval and logs it (structured), so GPU utilization — the real bottleneck — is visible per run. (Optionally export as custom Cloud Monitoring metrics.)
3. Both should ride the same config-gated debug/trace sink as #520 (off by default), and compose with the #526 speculative-spin-up work (a warm-and-wait worker should report "warming" vs "processing").

## Relation
Sub-slice of #520 (step-level tracing, server + UI). This bug is the **runtime-visibility** cut specifically: (a) progress %, (b) GPU/CPU/RAM — the two things that were missing when watching the live CUJ.

_Filed at owner request from the 2026-07-08 live CUJ (GX010151, 5.81 GiB)._

### 326. assistant (2026-07-08T08:29:29.423Z)

On it — filing this as a focused, fix-ASAP bug. But let me be straight about your "reuse the same L4 instance" hope, because it splits into two parts:

**The copy-elimination is achievable now; literal same-instance reuse is not (yet).** Here's why:
- **Literally reusing one L4 instance** across detect → *(user reviews/selects rallies)* → compile means keeping the GPU **warm across the human-review gap** (minutes). Cloud Run **Jobs** are scale-to-zero and have no warm pool — the detect instance is gone by the time you click Compile. Holding one instance warm the whole time = the **always-on L4** model you deliberately deferred (#524). So *that exact framing* is the always-on path.
- **But you don't need instance reuse to kill the ~1m38s.** The compile worker currently does a **full 5.8 GiB pull** (`acquire_local_input` → local `/tmp`) even though it only stitches **18 short clips (~80 s of footage)**. It already GCSFuse-mounts the bucket — so it can **read just those 18 ranges** (tens of MB) via the mount / GCS range reads instead of copying the whole file. That eliminates the roundtrip copy **without any warm-instance infra**, and should turn ~1m38s into seconds. That's the ASAP fix.

Filing it now:

### 327. assistant (2026-07-08T08:29:51.690Z)

**bug / perf — fix ASAP.** Measured on the 2026-07-08 compile→L4 CUJ (rev `rally-control-plane-00020-dtg`, worker `rally-worker-fdbs2`, clip GX010151 = 5.81 GiB / 554 s 4K). Related: #524 (data-movement), #521 (compile→L4), #534 (visibility).

## Problem — the compile worker re-downloads the FULL source it barely reads
The L4 compile worker pulls the **entire 5.81 GiB source** to local `/tmp` (`orchestration._execute_compile` → `storage.acquire_local_input(video["filepath"], …)`) before stitching — even though this reel only uses **18 short clips (~80 s of footage)** out of a 554 s source. Measured cost of that pull:

```
08:11:56  [worker] compile: stitching
08:13:34  Source video duration: 554.49s; Trimming 18 segments…   ← ~1m38s just acquiring the source
```

**~1m38s of the 5m17s compile wall is a full-file copy we don't need.** And it's the **4th** time the source moves in one run: upload→bucket, then bucket→(CP validate, detect worker, **compile worker**). The compile worker already **GCSFuse-mounts** the bucket (`/gcs`) — it just doesn't use the mount for the source; it copies the whole file first.

## Goal
No roundtrip copies on compile: the stitcher should read **only the clip ranges it needs**, not the whole file.

## Approaches (most-achievable first — no warm infra required)
1. **Stream the clip ranges via the GCSFuse mount / GCS range reads (PRIMARY, ASAP).** ffmpeg trims each clip by seeking to its `[start,end]` — for 18 clips that's ~tens of MB of a 5.8 GiB file. Point the stitcher at the mounted `/gcs/...` source (or a range-reading input) instead of `acquire_local_input`'s full fetch. **Benchmark GCSFuse random-seek reads vs the full pull** (tune `gcsfuse` read-ahead / the `Read` block size; random seeks can be inefficient, but reading ~80 s beats copying 5.8 GiB by ~70×). Fall back to the full pull if the mount read is slower on a given box.
2. **Co-locate detect + compile / instant-preview reel.** Auto-stitch a default reel (all rallies) on the **same** exec that just detected (source already local), then re-compile only if the user edits the selection. One exec, zero extra copies for the common case — at the cost of a UX change (a default reel appears before manual selection).
3. **Literal same-instance reuse across detect→review→compile** = keeping one L4 warm across the human-review gap = the **always-on L4** model (deferred in #524). Fully eliminates copies (source stays on local disk the whole run) but needs warm infra — **not** the ASAP path; tracked under #524.

## Acceptance
- On this clip, compile-worker source acquisition drops from **~1m38s to ~seconds** (a subset stitch reads ~clip-ranges, not the full file).
- No full-file copy on the compile path for a subset stitch; full-pull retained as a fallback.
- Numbers captured (before/after) the way §9 did.

## Evidence
Report `docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_validation-latency_2026-07-07.md` §9 + this run: compile wall 5m17s = ~23 s cold-start + **~1m38s source pull** + 2m31s NVENC trim (18 clips @ ~8.4 s) + ~6 s concat + ~29 s reel upload.

_Filed at owner request from the 2026-07-08 compile→L4 CUJ — fix ASAP._

| **compile→L4 dispatches** | ✅ `/api/compile` dispatched a `kind='compile'` Cloud Run Job exec (`rally-worker-fdbs2`), NOT the in-process CP stitch |
| **NVENC engaged** | ✅ `encoder=h264_nvenc` → `encoding clips with h264_nvenc (hardware)` (not the libx264 fallback) |
| **Stitch speedup** | ✅ **~67 s/clip → ~8.4 s/clip (~8×)** — 18 clips trimmed in **2m31s** (est. ~20 min on the old 2-vCPU CP path) |
| `trust_submit_video_id` (#524) | ✅ `trusting submit-time video_id=af2cd79a… — skipped the drain re-hash` |
| Validation placement | ✅ stayed on the control-plane (the authoritative #512/#513 gate), as configured |

**Bottom line:** the release does what it set out to. The reel stitch — the ~13-min control-plane bottleneck that motivated #521 — now runs on the L4 with hardware NVENC at **~8× the per-clip throughput**, dispatched as its own Job. On a 5.8 GiB / 9.2-min clip with 18 selected clips the full compile took **5m17s**, of which the stitch itself was ~2.5 min; the rest is fixed overhead (a **~1m38s full-source re-download** → now #535, plus a 1.75 GiB reel upload). `trust_submit_video_id` skipped the redundant re-hash. Validation stayed on the CP by design.

## 2. Resources
- **Image:** `asia-southeast1-docker.pkg.dev/gen-lang-client-0306026826/rally/rally-worker@sha256:7b62a8df2dc6097aa228a695de73d4c0bc4fe8008a08a8024c02a5376cda6943`, built from `origin/master @ 304af7b` (Cloud Build `cloudbuild.yaml`, 12m57s). Carries #525 (compile→L4 + `phase_placement`), #527/#528 (#521 follow-ups), #530 (#524 trust-video_id).
- **Control-plane:** `rally-control-plane` service, rev **`rally-control-plane-00020-dtg`** (100% traffic), `DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving`, 2 vCPU / 8 GiB, edge-auth on. Deployed via the committed manifest generator (`gen_service_yaml.py --edge-auth on --image <digest>` → `services replace`).
- **Worker:** `rally-worker` Cloud Run Job, same digest, 8 vCPU / 32 GiB / 1× L4, GCSFuse mount of `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving`.
- **Config (cloud-serving preset — the wins, on by default):** `phase_placement.compile=cloud_run_l4`, `cloud_stitch_encoder=h264_nvenc`, `segment→cloud_run_l4` (via `compute_target=cloud_run`), `trust_submit_video_id` (default). `validation` deliberately `control_plane`.
- **Corpus:** `GX010151.MP4` (**5.81 GiB** = 6,240,047,860 B; **554.49 s** ≈ 9.2 min, 4K GoPro), video_id `af2cd79a7c8df448501151f797338b57`. Reel → `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/highlights/af2cd79a…/second-ks-highlight-reel.mp4` (**1.75 GiB**).

## 3. Live CUJ timeline (UTC, rev `00020`)

| Event | Timestamp | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/process` 202 | 07:55:07 | 5.81 GiB upload staged |
| `trusting submit-time video_id … skipped re-hash` (#524) | 07:56:38 | ✅ no drain re-hash |
| `Running validations` | 07:56:38 | on the CP (as configured) |
| `Video passed checks` | 07:59:21 | **validation wall = ~162 s (2m42s)**, PASSED |
| dispatch → detect exec `rally-worker-956vm` | 07:59:23 | segment→L4 |
| `[worker] pulled … in.mp4` | 08:01:24 | **~2 min cold-start** — FIRST exec after image push ("container image import"), one-time |
| `[worker] validation SKIPPED (--skip-validation)` | 08:01:36 | CP is the gate (#513) |
| `Phase 2 (WASB) completed in 460.5s` | 08:09:31 | **40 candidate windows**; 16 618 traj pts (6 758 visible) |
| `status=success` | 08:09:40 | 40 rallies ingested |
| `POST /api/compile` (18 clips) | 08:11:31 | owner selected 18 of 40 |
| dispatched **COMPILE** exec `rally-worker-fdbs2` | 08:11:32 | ✅ compile→L4 (not in-process) |
| compile worker start | 08:11:55 | `clips=18 encoder=h264_nvenc` |
| `Source video duration: 554.49s; Trimming 18…` | 08:13:34 | **~1m38s source pull** (full 5.8 GiB → local; #535) |
| `encoding clips with h264_nvenc (hardware)` | 08:13:35 | ✅ NVENC, not libx264 |
| Trimming Segment #1…#18/18 | 08:13:35 → 08:16:06 | **2m31s, ~8.4 s/clip** |
| reel saved (1794.5 MB) + upload | 08:16:12 → 08:16:41 | ~6 s concat + ~29 s reel upload |
| CP: `COMPILE … done rc=0` | 08:16:49 | **compile wall = 317 s (5m17s)** |

## 4. The stitch win (compile→L4 + NVENC)

| | Old path (CP, libx264, 2 vCPU) | This run (L4, NVENC, 8 vCPU) |
|---|---|---|
| Per-clip trim | ~67 s/clip (§9 baseline) | **~8.4 s/clip** |
| 18 clips, trim only | ~20 min (extrapolated) | **2m31s** |
| Encoder | libx264 (CPU) | **h264_nvenc (hardware)** |
| Reel | — | 1.75 GiB from 18 clips / 554 s source |

**Compile wall (5m17s) breakdown:** ~23 s worker cold-start (2nd exec, no image-import) + **~1m38s full-source pull (#535)** + **2m31s NVENC trim** + ~6 s concat + ~29 s reel upload. The stitch itself is the win; the ~1m38s pull is pure overhead (§6/#535). For scale: all 40 rallies on the old CP path ≈ **~45 min**; the 18-clip subset here stitched in ~2.5 min.

## 5. Worker resource utilization (during detect, via Monitoring API)
- **GPU:** **not observable** — `run.googleapis.com/container/gpu/utilization` 404s and the worker doesn't self-report `nvidia-smi`. The bottleneck box is the one we're blind to (→ #534).
- **CPU:** ~25% → steady **~14–15% of 8 vCPU** — low; WASB is GPU-bound, CPU just feeds the GPU.
- **RAM:** **~21% of 32 GiB (~6.7 GiB)** — the 5.81 GiB clip in RAM-backed `/tmp` + the model; comfortable, no pressure.

## 6. Honest limits / follow-ups
- **No progress % + no GPU/CPU/RAM visibility (#534).** The detector runs as one opaque pass (start + done, no heartbeat); the SPA just spins for the multi-minute run — the main reason the compile "felt slow". GPU util isn't captured at all. Filed as **#534** (runtime-visibility slice of #520).
- **Compile worker re-downloads the FULL 5.8 GiB source (~1m38s) for an ~80 s reel (#535).** It only needs the 18 clip ranges; reading them via the GCSFuse mount / range reads (no full copy) should turn ~1m38s into seconds — filed **#535** (fix ASAP). The source now moves 4× per run (upload→bucket, bucket→{validate, detect, compile}); full copy-elimination end-to-end is the #524 always-on path (deferred).
- **First-exec cold-start (~2 min)** was the one-time "container image import" after the image push; steady-state execs are faster (the compile exec `fdbs2` cold-start was ~23 s).
- **`skip_ai_handoff=true`** (secret-free alpha) → the 40 "rallies" are candidate windows (no Gemini refine); the reel is a real branded stitch of the 18 selected windows. Detection + compile paths are fully exercised.
- **Validation stayed on the CP** (the authoritative #512/#513 gate) — moving it to the L4 (`phase_placement.validation=cloud_run_l4`, kills the ~30 s CP re-download) is a config flip left off by design; owner-confirmed.

## 7. Cost
One detect L4 exec (`956vm`, ~10 min incl. cold-start) + one compile L4 exec (`fdbs2`, ~5 min) + the CP CPU (validation ~162 s; compile now OFF the CP) ≈ **~$0.5–1** (L4-dominated; the ~13-min CP stitch is gone). Storage/egress: the 1.75 GiB reel + 5.8 GiB upload, intra-region.

## 8. Rollout state + rollback
- **Live** on rev `00020-dtg` + worker digest `7b62a8df`. `origin/master` has since moved to `5880c26` (post-deploy, NOT in prod).
- **Rollback (verified anchors):** CP → `gcloud run services update-traffic rally-control-plane --region asia-southeast1 --to-revisions rally-control-plane-00019-96f=100`; worker → `gcloud run jobs update rally-worker --region asia-southeast1 --image …/rally-worker@sha256:c6393d27b4ae493ccf713ed0aa061938e76eae7151d6541466dcfa521f638e1d`.
- ⚠ Both surfaces deploy off the mutable `:latest` tag — release-process/config-mgmt hardening tracked in **#532**.

## 9. Verdict
✅ **Validated in prod.** `compile→L4` dispatches correctly and NVENC engages, cutting the reel stitch **~8× per clip (67→8.4 s)** and taking the ~13-min control-plane bottleneck off the CP entirely; `trust_submit_video_id` skips the redundant re-hash; validation stays the CP gate. Residual latency on this worst-case 6 GB clip is the missing progress UI (#534) and the ~1m38s compile-source re-download (#535, fix ASAP) — both filed. Release is good; #532 hardens how future releases ship.

## What changes on your next CUJ
- The CP **delegates validation to the L4 worker** (32 GiB): the worker validates *and* detects in one exec, so the **validator decode moves off the 2-vCPU CP** entirely. The dispatch no longer carries `--skip-validation`; the worker is the gate now, and a clip that fails validation comes back as a **failed Job (rc≠0)** rather than an upfront CP reject.

## Honest residual (so you're not surprised)
As I found in the deployed code, the CP still calls `acquire_local_input` **unconditionally** — so the 6.52 GiB file **still downloads into the CP's RAM-backed `/tmp`** to compute the video_id (the object has no MD5, so the streamed-id shortcut can't apply). So CP peak memory ≈ **file (6.52) + process (~0.5) ≈ 7 GiB of the 8 GiB** — validation→L4 removed the *decode* on top, which is the part that was pushing it toward the edge. The prior 5.81 GiB clip fit *with* decode, so this should fit, but it's tight.

**If you want a hard guarantee** (zero OOM risk), pick one and I'll do it in ~1 min:
- **(a) Bump CP memory to 16 GiB** — belt-and-suspenders; note this forces Cloud Run to **4 vCPU** (a slightly bigger/cheaper-per-run CP), reversible.
- **(b) Proceed as-is** — validation→L4 alone should fit (~7 < 8); if it ever OOMs mid-run, that's the signal to do (a).

The *real* fix is that the CP shouldn't download the full file at all when validation+segment are both delegated — that's the #524/#535 data-movement family (I can fold "CP skips `acquire_local_input` when fully delegated" into #535).

Two notes for the record: this validation→L4 flip is a **manifest `config.json` override, not the versioned preset** — so it's config-drift (exactly #532's point) and won't survive a fresh preset-based deploy; the permanent home is `presets.py`. Rollback to validation-on-CP = redeploy rev `00020-dtg`.

Your call on (a) vs (b) — otherwise you're clear to run the CUJ on `third-ksg-cuj-run.MP4`.

### 398. assistant (2026-07-08T09:01:58.279Z)

**bug — cost + correctness.** Observed live 2026-07-08 (rev `rally-control-plane-00020/00021`). Related: #515 (crash-without-marker fast-fail — a *different* path), #521 (compile also dispatches now).

## Symptom
**Canceling a run in the SPA does NOT stop the dispatched Cloud Run Job worker execution — it keeps running to completion on the L4, burning GPU.**

Reproduced: owner started a run on `third-ksg-cuj-run.MP4` (6.52 GiB), then **canceled it in the SPA**. The dispatched detection exec `rally-worker-8gqkv` kept going regardless:
```
08:55:14  [worker] pulled …/third-ksg-cuj-run.MP4 -> in.mp4
08:55:47  Running native WASB inference (device=cuda) on in.mp4 …   ← still running after the SPA cancel
```
It was still `runningCount=1` minutes later and only stopped when manually `gcloud run jobs executions cancel rally-worker-8gqkv`'d (~09:01). ~5–6 min of L4 wasted; left unattended it would have run the full ~7–10 min detection on the 6.5 GiB clip and written outputs — a wasted ~$0.5 exec (worse for the compile path, and worse under concurrency).

## Root cause (observed)
The cancel path (SPA → control-plane job) does not propagate to the **dispatched Cloud Run Job execution**. The CP stops bucket-polling the done-marker / marks its job row canceled, but nothing tells the L4 worker exec to stop — so it runs to completion, orphaned.

## Ask
When a run/job is canceled (or otherwise abandoned), **cancel the dispatched worker execution**:
- The CP already knows the exec name — it logs it at dispatch (`compute.cloud_run: dispatched job=rally-worker exec=…/executions/<name>; bucket-polling …/_dispatch/<token>.done`) and tracks it while polling. On cancel, call `executions.cancel` (Cloud Run Jobs API) on that exec (best-effort; ignore if already terminal).
- Do the same for the **compile** dispatch (`_dispatch_compile_remote`, exec `rally-worker-<...>` polling `_compile/<token>.done`) — #521 added a second dispatch path with the same exposure.
- Bound it: a cancel should also stop the CP-side bucket-poll promptly (so a canceled run doesn't keep polling for ~65 min either).

## Acceptance
- Canceling a run in the SPA cancels the dispatched detect/compile exec within seconds (verify `status.cancelledCount=1`, `runningCount=0`), not run-to-completion.
- No orphaned L4 execution after a user cancel; the CP job row + the exec agree.
- A cancel of an already-terminal exec is a no-op (no error).

## Evidence
Exec `rally-worker-8gqkv` (detect, `third-ksg-cuj-run.MP4`): dispatched 08:53, inference 08:55:47, still running until a manual `executions cancel` at ~09:01 (`cancelledCount=1`, completion 09:01:06). Filed at owner request from the 2026-07-08 session.

### 413. assistant (2026-07-08T09:06:03.064Z)

## Detailed validation criteria (abandoned / canceled execution)

Confirmed live 2026-07-08: canceling a run in the SPA left the dispatched detect exec `rally-worker-8gqkv` running WASB inference on the L4 for ~5–6 min until a manual `gcloud run jobs executions cancel`. The fix must make cancel propagate to the worker exec. Concrete, testable acceptance below.

### A. Detect-path cancel (primary)
1. **Cancel mid-detect → exec stops fast.** Start a cloud run; once the detect exec is `runningCount=1`, cancel via the SPA (`DELETE`/cancel on the job). **PASS:** within ≤ 15 s the exec reaches `status.cancelledCount=1`, `runningCount=0`, `completionTime` set; no `report.json`/`intervals.csv` written to `…/outputs/<video_id>/`.
2. **No GPU billed after cancel.** **PASS:** the exec's `completionTime − cancel_timestamp ≤ ~15 s`; no GPU-seconds accrue past the cancel (spot-check via the exec runtime / Monitoring). Left unfixed, a 6.5 GiB clip burns the full ~7–10 min detect.
3. **CP job row agrees.** **PASS:** `/api/jobs/<id>` reports a terminal `canceled` (not `processing`/`success`), and the CP stops bucket-polling the `_dispatch/<token>.done` marker promptly (≤ 15 s), NOT after the ~65 min `remote_poll_max_wait_sec`.

### B. Compile-path cancel (#521 added a 2nd dispatch)
4. **Cancel mid-compile → compile exec stops.** Start a compile; cancel while the `rally-worker-<…>` compile exec runs. **PASS:** same as A1 for the compile exec; no reel mirrored to `…/highlights/<video_id>/`, CP stops polling `_compile/<token>.done`.

### C. Edge cases / robustness
5. **Cancel-before-dispatch** (during CP validation, before any exec): **PASS:** job canceled cleanly; **no** worker exec is spawned afterwards.
6. **Cancel of an already-terminal exec** (succeeded/failed just before the cancel): **PASS:** no-op, no error, no state corruption, CP job stays terminal.
7. **Idempotent double-cancel:** two cancels for the same run → **PASS:** second is a no-op.
8. **Concurrency isolation:** cancel run A while run B is dispatched → **PASS:** only A's exec is canceled; B's exec + job untouched.
9. **Best-effort, non-fatal:** if `executions.cancel` errors (already gone / API blip), the CP cancel still completes and logs it; never a 500 to the SPA.

### D. Observability (ties #520/#534)
10. The cancel + exec-cancel is logged with the exec name and outcome (e.g. `[cancel] canceled exec rally-worker-<name> (rc=canceled)`), so an operator can confirm no orphan.

### Implementation note
The CP already knows the exec name — it logs it at dispatch (`compute.cloud_run: dispatched job=rally-worker exec=…/executions/<name>; bucket-polling …/_dispatch/<token>.done`) and tracks it while polling. Cancel should call the Cloud Run Jobs `executions.cancel` on that tracked name (detect + compile), then stop the poll. Regression-test with a fake `CloudRunJobClient` that records `cancel(exec_name)` calls, mirroring `tests/test_api_cloud_dispatch.py`.

### 429. assistant (2026-07-08T09:22:58.434Z)

You're right to be annoyed — "starting up" is hiding **~5.5 minutes** of real work with zero granularity. Here's what it's actually doing (POST at `09:15:31` → real detection at `09:21:13`):

| "Starting up" sub-phase | Wall | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| CP download + hash | 09:15:31 → 09:17:33 (**~2m0s**) | CP pulls the whole **6.52 GiB** to hash the video_id — then delegates validation to L4 |
| Worker cold-start + pull | 09:17:35 → 09:19:50 (**~2m15s**) | exec schedule + GCSFuse + **another** 6.52 GiB pull to the worker |
| Worker WASB init | 09:19:50 → 09:21:13 (**~1m23s**) | torch/cuda env init |
| **Real detection begins** | **09:21:13** | WASB inference on cuda |

The single most wasteful chunk is the **first one**: with validation *and* segment both on the L4, the control-plane is a pure dispatcher — it does **not** need the file — yet it still pulls all 6.52 GiB just to compute the video_id (because this directly-staged object has **no MD5**, and the code downloads unconditionally anyway at `orchestration.py:814`). That's ~2 min of "starting up" for nothing. The file is then pulled **again** by the worker. 

This is exactly the cluster we've been filing:
- **#526** (speculative L4 spin-up at upload-start) → hides the worker cold-start.
- **#535** (read clip ranges, don't full-copy) → the source-movement tax.
- **#534/#520** → the opaque "starting up" itself (no progress/stage detail).
- **Not yet filed:** *the CP shouldn't download at all when validation+segment are delegated* — the ~2-min redundant CP pull above. That's a distinct, very fixable waste.

Want me to file that last one as its own bug? It's the cleanest 2-minute win of the four.

### 432. assistant (2026-07-08T09:25:14.922Z)

**bug / perf — fix ASAP.** Measured live 2026-07-08 (rev `rally-control-plane-00022-29w`, validation+segment+compile all `cloud_run_l4`, clip `third-ksg-cuj-run.MP4` = 6.52 GiB). Sibling of #524 (data-movement), #535 (compile source pull), #526 (spin-up), #534/#520 (visibility).

## Problem — the control-plane downloads the whole video it never uses
When `phase_placement.validation` **and** `segment` are both `cloud_run_l4`, the control-plane is a **pure dispatcher** — it doesn't validate, detect, or stitch, so it needs **no local copy** of the video. Yet `_execute_processing` calls `storage.acquire_local_input(req.video_path, …)` **unconditionally** (`backend/orchestration.py:814`) and pulls the **entire file** into the CP's RAM-backed `/tmp`, purely to compute the `video_id` via `compute_video_id(local_video)`.

**Measured cost:** for the 6.52 GiB clip this was **~2 minutes** of the "starting up" phase (`POST /api/process 09:15:31 → dispatch 09:17:33`) — before the worker even starts, and the worker then pulls the **same** 6.52 GiB again. It scales with file size, so it's worst on exactly the long GoPro clips this product targets. It's also the reason the CP needed a **16 GiB** bump (rev 00022) — the file sits in RAM-`/tmp` for no functional reason.

## Why it happens (two prongs)
1. `acquire_local_input` at line 814 is unconditional — it runs even when nothing on the CP needs the file. The #524/#530 `trust_submit_video_id` logic (~line 820) only skips the **re-hash**, NOT the **download**.
2. The cheap id path (`submit_time_video_id`, one `stat` for the object's stored MD5) can't fire here because this **directly-staged object has no MD5** (a composite/parallel `gcloud storage cp` upload stores only crc32c). So it falls back to download+hash.

## Ask
Make the CP **not download the source when it doesn't need it**:
- **Gate `acquire_local_input`**: only fetch a local copy when a CP-side consumer actually needs it (CP validation, CP segmentation, or a hash with no cheaper source). When validation+segment are delegated AND a `video_id` is resolvable without the bytes, **skip the download** → the CP stays a thin dispatcher (dispatch within seconds).
- **Resolve `video_id` cheaply**: prefer the object's stored MD5 (`submit_time_video_id`, one stat); else a **streaming/range hash** (constant memory, no full local copy); only fall back to a full download as a last resort, with a clear log.
- **Staging fix (adjacent):** ensure large/staged uploads get an MD5 — disable parallel-composite upload for the staging path (or backfill the MD5) so `submit_time_video_id` works. (crc32c-only composite objects defeat the cheap id path.)

## Validation criteria
1. **No CP download when fully delegated + id resolvable:** with `validation=segment=cloud_run_l4` and an object whose id is resolvable (MD5 stat or trusted submit id), `_execute_processing` does **not** call `acquire_local_input`; CP `/tmp` stays small (assert no ~GiB temp file); **POST→dispatch ≤ a few seconds** (not minutes). For the 6.5 GiB clip: ~2 min → ~seconds.
2. **Correct id:** the `video_id` used equals `compute_video_id(file)` byte-for-byte (regression vector).
3. **Streaming-hash fallback:** when only a hash-by-read is possible, memory stays flat (no full file in `/tmp`); verify peak RSS/`/tmp` ≪ file size.
4. **No regression when CP-side work is placed on the CP:** if validation or segment is `control_plane`, the CP still fetches the local file as today (byte-identical behavior).
5. **CP memory:** with the download gone, the CP no longer needs 16 GiB for large clips (can revert to 8 GiB once verified).
6. **Observability:** log which path was taken (`[api] video_id via stored-md5 / streaming-hash / full-download`), ties #534.

## Evidence
Rev 00022 run: `POST 09:15:31` → CP pulls 6.52 GiB → `validation delegated … dispatching 09:17:33` (~2m0s) → worker pulls the SAME 6.52 GiB `09:17:35→09:19:50`. Object `third-ksg-cuj-run.MP4` has no md5_hash (composite upload). Filed at owner request from the 2026-07-08 retry CUJ.

### 448. assistant (2026-07-08T09:32:05.940Z)

## Update (2026-07-08 retry CUJ) — correction + make utilization first-class

### Correction: GPU **is** observable (I had the metric name wrong)
The earlier "GPU utilization 404 / not observable" was a mistake — I queried the **singular** `run.googleapis.com/container/gpu/utilization`. The real metrics exist and are **plural**:
- `run.googleapis.com/container/gpu/utilizations` — GPU compute %
- `run.googleapis.com/container/gpu/memory_utilizations` — VRAM %
- `run.googleapis.com/container/gpu/memory_usages` — VRAM bytes

So GPU telemetry is in Cloud Monitoring; the gap is that **it's buried there** (wrong-named in one place, and not surfaced anywhere an operator or the SPA can see without hand-querying the API). The runlog `DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_compile-on-L4_2026-07-08.md` §5 has the stale "not observable" claim → should be corrected.

### Measured this run (worker `rally-worker-gvqzv`, L4, 6.52 GiB clip, WASB detect)
| Resource | p99 | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **GPU compute** | **100%** | fully saturated — healthy, the GPU is the bottleneck |
| **GPU VRAM** | **97% (23.4 / 24 GiB)** | near-max → **CUDA-OOM risk** on bigger batch/resolution; worth an alert + tuning `indexing.wasb.batch_size` |
| Host RAM | 24% of 32 GiB | comfortable (the 6.5 GiB clip in `/tmp` + model + decode) |
| CPU | ~15% of 8 vCPU | low — CPU just feeds the GPU |

### First-class utilization reporting (owner ask)
Make GPU/VRAM/CPU/RAM a first-class signal, not a Cloud-Monitoring dig:
1. **Worker self-reports** on an interval (e.g. every 10–15 s) during validate/detect/compile: GPU compute %, **VRAM used/%**, host RAM %, CPU % — via `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=utilization.gpu,memory.used,memory.total` / NVML + `/proc`. Emit as a structured log line and (optionally) a custom Cloud Monitoring metric.
2. **Surface to the SPA** alongside the progress heartbeat (this issue's item 1) so a run shows live GPU/VRAM/CPU/RAM, not a spinner.
3. **VRAM guard/alert:** warn when VRAM > ~90% (near CUDA-OOM); today WASB sits at **97%** on 4K, so this is not hypothetical.

### Also: worker-side VALIDATION is invisible
With `phase_placement.validation=cloud_run_l4`, the worker runs the full validator suite (`worker/__main__.py:289`, confirmed by code + the absence of the `--skip-validation` branch), but **emits no logline** — the `Running validations… / passed checks` banner lives only on the control-plane path (`orchestration`). So when validation runs on the L4 it's silent (no "validating", no verdict, no timing). Add worker-side `validating… / passed checks (Ns)` loglines so the gate is observable when delegated.

_Validation criteria: a delegated-validation run shows a start + verdict + duration in logs; a live run surfaces GPU compute + VRAM% (with the ~90% alert) + CPU + host RAM; the runlog's "not observable" line is corrected._

### 461. assistant (2026-07-08T09:38:57.617Z)

Reel's done — the retry **completed end-to-end**: `third-ksg-cuj-run-ks-highlight-reel.mp4`, **1.94 GiB**, at 09:36:06 (`rc=0`). So the whole new stack (validation→L4 + 16 GiB CP + compile→L4 + NVENC) is validated on the worst-case **6.52 GiB / 10.4-min / 44-rally** clip, no OOM.

## Where & how the reel is generated (deployed today)
**Where:** on the **L4 compile worker** (`98gqk`, dispatched by the CP via compile→L4), then mirror-uploaded to `…/highlights/473cea0f…/…reel.mp4`.

**How (the deployed mechanism, `304af7b`):** it's **sequential, one clip at a time** — for each of the 16 selected rallies, `_trim_one_segment` runs an ffmpeg subprocess that seeks to `[start,end]`, **burns the watermark (`-vf` overlay), and NVENC re-encodes** it to a temp clip; then all clips are joined with `concat -c copy` (stream-copy, cheap). This run: **5m39s compile** = ~1m49s source pull + **2m26s trimming (16 clips @ ~9 s each)** + ~6 s concat + ~38 s reel upload.

## Is it the best mechanism? Your parallelism instinct — mostly right, with a better lever already in-flight
Your instinct is correct that the **per-clip extraction is the expensive, parallelizable part** (each clip is an independent seek+encode). But three things sharpen it:

1. **The bigger win isn't parallelizing the re-encodes — it's not doing N of them.** There's already **#531 "single-pass concat-filter stitch"** merged to master (**not deployed** — the live image predates it) that stitches the whole reel in **one ffmpeg pass** (concat filter + a single watermark pass) instead of 16 per-clip re-encodes. That *removes* the work rather than parallelizing it. This is the cleaner path and it exists — **deploy + measure it first.**
2. **The NVENC ceiling caps naive parallelism.** The L4 has a **single hardware NVENC engine** — running 16 encodes at once time-slices it, so a parallel pool gets you maybe **~2–4×**, not 16×, before the encoder saturates. Real, but bounded.
3. **Your specific "two videos → merge" is a *weaker* form of clip-level parallelism.** The concat/merge is already a near-free stream-copy, so splitting into two and merging adds a stage without helping the bottleneck. A **flat parallel pool of per-clip trims + one concat** is simpler and strictly more parallel — and it's moot anyway if #531's single-pass lands (there are no separate clip files to parallelize; ffmpeg's own filtergraph threading does it).

And the honest kicker: on *this* run the stitch wasn't even the biggest compile cost — the **~1m49s source re-download** (#535) was, and that's not about the stitch mechanism at all.

**So the priority order for compile latency:** (1) **deploy #531** (single-pass — removes the N re-encodes), (2) **#535** (read clip ranges, kill the ~1m49s full-source pull), (3) parallel per-clip pool *only if* per-clip survives #531 and NVENC has headroom.

Want me to build+deploy the current master (which includes #531's single-pass stitch) and re-measure the compile, so we see the single-pass win live? That's the concrete next step — and it'd also fold in the other post-`304af7b` merges.

### 464. assistant (2026-07-08T09:40:15.655Z)

**feature / product direction.** A headless, programmatic ("agentic") way to drive the full pipeline for a **batch** of videos — callable by a human script OR their AI agent — as an alternative to the manual, per-video web SPA. Umbrella for the efficiency work: #524/#526/#535/#539 (data-movement/latency), #534/#520 (observability), #532 (release/config).

## Motivation
The web SPA is one-video-at-a-time and manual (upload → process → **review** → click compile → download), and there is **no headless path at all**. Concretely, in the 2026-07-08 session an AI agent could **not** run a single job on the user's behalf:
- `/api/process` is gated behind a **browser** Cloudflare-Access JWT — there is **no service-token** path for programmatic callers.
- There is no batch API/SDK/CLI — each video is hand-driven in the SPA.
- Compile requires a human to select clips and click — no auto-compile.

So a request like **"work with the Khelsutra repo and get me the reels for all the videos in this local folder"** is not automatable today.

## Vision
A single call/command — e.g. `khelsutra reels ./folder/ --compute hosted` (CLI) or an SDK — that, for **every** video in a folder/glob: stages → processes (validate+detect) → **auto-compiles** a reel (per a policy) → downloads it, with concurrency, resume, and a summary manifest. Designed to be invoked by a person **or their agent**. Two compute targets:
- **Hosted:** run against Khelsutra's infra (the control-plane + L4 worker).
- **BYO-cloud:** the repo code spins up the worker in the **user's own** cloud project (the `deploy/cloudrun` / `deploy/gcp` / `deploy/digitalocean` scripts already exist), and the SDK points at it — so they run *their* code on *their* GPU.

The primitives already exist — this is a headless surface + auth + batch orchestration over them: `/api/upload` (+ direct-stage), `/api/process`, `/api/jobs/<id>`, `/api/compile`, `/api/highlights`, and the worker CLI `python -m backend.worker infer|compile` (the "works the repo code" piece).

## Requirements (phased)
**Phase 1 — headless MVP (hosted):**
1. **Service-token auth** — accept a CF-Access **service token** (`CF-Access-Client-Id`/`CF-Access-Client-Secret`) on `/api/*`, so non-browser callers authenticate. (This is the #1 blocker — direct `run.app` POST → 401 today.)
2. **A CLI + typed SDK (Python/TS)** wrapping the endpoints: `stage/upload`, `process`, `wait(job)`, `compile`, `download`.
3. **Auto-compile policy** — produce a reel without human review: `--rallies all|top:N|min-shots:K` (default "all"), so the whole flow is non-interactive.
4. **Batch driver** — iterate a folder/glob, run N videos concurrently, emit a **manifest** (`{video, video_id, rallies, reel_uri, status, timings}`), resumable/idempotent (skip already-processed via the video_id cache).

**Phase 2 — BYO-cloud:**
5. `khelsutra provision --cloud gcp|do --project …` scripts the worker deploy into the caller's own project (reuse `deploy/*`); the SDK targets that endpoint. Teardown included ($0 after).
6. Local direct-stage path (skip the chunked SPA upload) for large files, with an MD5 so the cheap `video_id` path works (see #539).

## Validation criteria
- **One command, whole folder:** `khelsutra reels ./folder/` produces one reel per video with **no SPA and no manual clicks**; exit code + manifest reflect per-video success/failure.
- **Headless auth:** the same works from a server/agent with only a service token (no browser session).
- **Batch:** N videos run concurrently (bounded), with per-video progress (ties #534) and a final summary.
- **Idempotent:** re-running skips videos already reeled (video_id cache), only (re)does the missing ones.
- **BYO-cloud:** the identical SDK call runs against a worker deployed in a fresh user-owned project and tears it down to $0.
- **Agent-usable:** documented such that an AI coding agent can call it end-to-end from a repo checkout (the thing that was impossible this session).

## Evidence
2026-07-08 session: the agent had to have the owner drive the SPA for **every** run (validation-latency CUJ ×3, compile→L4, retries) — no service-token, no batch API, compile requires manual selection. Filed at owner request.
